6. **THE MAN WITH THE BAD EAR, thirty-one, who keeps Tallowgate's column of nineteen dead, and who asked for his own name to be put on a slate upstairs and cannot get it taken off.** He is a person of Tallowgate and says the truest useless thing anybody says in the band: the column is not a lie, and it is the most useless thing anybody has ever said in this ward.
7. **THE WOMAN OF TWENTY-EIGHT WHO GOES TO DOORS ON HER OWN ACCOUNT**, nineteen years in the city, nine days of chalk, a bundle of string, a slate, a promise from him that he will say nothing to anybody, and a method she states in her own mouth in answer to a question: if the ward knew a woman of twenty-eight was doing it, the first thing that would happen is a rota, and a rota is a list, and a list is the thing that comes before a bill. **SHE IS NOT NAMED IN THE FICTION AND HE DOES NOT NAME HER, AND THAT IS A PROMISE HE MADE IN A YARD.**
8. **A CLERK OF THIRTY IN THE PUBLIC ROOM WHO READ THE ORDER ALOUD TO A STRANGER, AND THE SAME CLERK IN THE CHAMBER UPSTAIRS WHO WROTE ILYAN'S NAME ON A SHEET OFF A DOCUMENT IN FRONT OF HIM, AND WHO LOOKED FOR A FORM TO TAKE IT OFF AND FOUND NONE.** He did not enjoy reading the order aloud to a stranger and he said so.
9. **A CLERK OF ABOUT FORTY AT THE END OF THE PUBLIC ROOM TABLE, whose job it is to write down what is said in that room, who put a fair hand into a book in about eleven minutes and got a figure wrong once and was corrected by a man at a rail who did not say his name.**
10. **A CARTER OF FIFTY OR SO WITH A BAD COUGH, four days on a road with a board the size of a door under a strap, who carried a map to this city because a city wrote and asked and paid him eighteen pence a day, and who read one line of it through the sacking and has no opinion about any of it.**
11. **A MAN OF SIXTY OR SO AT THE GATE WITH A BOOK ON HIS KNEES AND A COLUMN THAT WILL NOT BE FINISHED BEFORE THE LAST OF THE LIGHT, who let a stranger in with the line empty and told him he would be at that gate on his way out.**
12. **SERA QUILL, twenty-eight, licensed recordwright, in a room over a lock-and-ledger shop near the courts, four documents open and a lamp on, who is tired of being the place where a stranger's plans go to be right and says so in about nine sentences and is right.** Her family's claim of six years is unfiled, unresolved, said once, and not to be asked about again this year.
13. **TARIN KEEL, about fifty-two, at a rail outside the black building counting what comes through the door, because a fever closes the roads and a city that has closed its roads has never needed anybody to count what crosses them more.** He is right that a thing said in a corridor can be done by morning and has been in nine counties in nineteen years and has never been useful. He and Ilyan are allies with incompatible methods and neither is going to win and both are right.
14. **MAGISTRATE ANSA RELL, whose age IS NOT STATED IN THIS BAND AND MAY NOT BE INFERRED IN A HAND-OFF, referred to as Magistrate Rell, which is what Volume 02 did after Chapter 68.** She wrote the order in the third week. She left the sixth line empty on purpose and does not apologise and gives the best argument in the band. She is not a villain, she has hidden nothing, she is not softened, and her defeat in this volume produces a difficult ally and not a redeemed servant.
15. **AND EVERYBODY ELSE WHO IS ALIVE AND IS NOT IN THIS CITY.** The eleven Briar March threads at `state/continuity.md` §6 stand where they stood on Shelf day 225, and he is four hundred miles off and has been for ten days.

### 3. EVERY DOCUMENT THAT EXISTS IN THIS CITY, ONE EACH, AND WHERE IT IS

1. **THE SHELTER ORDER. Six lines. Up since the third week of the fever, on a notice board in the public room with a blank nine inches under it.** Its subject is the residents of the four low wards. Its condition is a declared fever. **Its burden is a person, and the whole of the line is: a person is inside the protection when the roll of the ward has the name of that person in it.** Its witness is the keeper of the roll, the master of the hall, and the person of the house in which the person sleeps. Its remedy is a board in a hall, water at a standpipe, and a wage at the end of the week, **and the order does not fix wages and the ward's book does and the ward's book has not been brought.** **Its sixth line is a line with a date in it and the date is not there, and in the margin beside the empty line there is one word, and the word is STANDING.** Read to a stranger in about eleven minutes by a clerk of thirty.
2. **THE ROLL OF TALLOWGATE, in paper. About fifteen hundred names. Kept by a woman of forty-four in a room at the back of a chandler's shop. THE ONLY COPY ANYBODY HAS OF WHAT SHE DID THIS WEEK.**
3. **THE PLATES. Thirteen hundred names, cut a hundred to a plate, on blackglass, in a yard at the low end of Tallowgate. Two hundred of them are on the paper and not on a plate because nobody could pay four shillings the hundred, and the two hundred that were cut in the last two years were paid for out of two women's own hands and neither would say what for.** About eleven hundred of the lines are above a polish line and two hundred are below it and the line is two years old.
4. **THE BLOCK IN THE GATEPOST.** A block the size of a door with a mark on the face that is not a name and not a date and not a figure anybody in the ward can read. **IT IS A WEIGHT. It was cut two years ago, in the same fortnight the plates were last cut right through, and it holds the weight of the ward, and a name cut onto a plate makes the plate heavier, and the block and the plate have been saying two different things ever since, and nobody reads the block.** Getting it right needs a meeting of the ward, a notice, a day, and about nine people in a room, and there has not been a meeting of Tallowgate since the autumn before the water came.
5. **THE FAIR HAND.** A clerk of forty's record of what a stranger said out loud in the public room in the morning of the seventh day of the band, which is `357` — **REPAIRED: this entry used to say *at the seventh hour of the second day of the band*, and the fair hand is written in Chapter 357, which is the seventh morning of his time in this city and not the second, and the chapter fixes the hour only as *after the fourth hour*, at `357:41`** —: three hundred names short, twelve shillings of plate, a pound three and sixpence for a date, and the difference between a bell keeper's year and a roll keeper's year. **It is in a book on a second floor and everybody in that building reads that book.**
6. **THE DRAFT. Four clauses and a half, on the back of a chandler's bill for candles, in a blunt pencil, in his coat.** ONE. A person is inside the protection from the moment the order stands. The paper and the plate are one record. A person may be entered by any person of the ward who can say the name, without fee. Somebody must go to the doors. **The fifth clause names the keeper of the roll and has a line struck through before it and not after it.** He did not file it and he did not tear it up.
7. **THE SHEET WITH HIS NAME ON IT.** One sheet, in a chamber upstairs, with two names on it for a witness to a question on the fourteenth, one of which is his. **HE PUT IT THERE HIMSELF AND THE CITY'S OWN CLERK WROTE IT OFF A DOCUMENT IN FRONT OF HIM, AND HE HAS NOT SEEN THE SHEET, AND THERE IS NO FORM THAT WILL TAKE A MAN OFF A PIECE OF PAPER ONCE IT IS ON THERE.**
8. **THE FOUR COLUMNS OF THE DEAD.** Nineteen in Tallowgate with nothing over the column and nothing at the foot of it and no heading at all. About a hundred and forty across the four wards. **NOT ADDED UP AND NOT ADDABLE.**
9. **THE BOOKS AT SIX HALL DOORS.** A line for every person who comes in. There is no line for a person who goes and no document that would make one.
10. **THE DOCKET.** A slate, not a paper, in a chamber upstairs, with a question on it: what the Bench is to do with a document it did not make, that says a thing is under this city and not found. **THE DATE IS THE FOURTEENTH.**
11. **THE MAP. One of nine copies, on the back of a flour mill's sack, sent from a county four hundred miles off because this city asked for one and paid the carriage. It arrived at the noon of the first day and came back at the last of the light with nine things on it and a clerk's mark in the corner that says a city received a document and does not say what it is going to do with it.** Six of the nine things are standing, one burned, two not found, and one of the two is this city. **THE WORD THE MAP USES FOR THEM HAS NOT BEEN SAID OUT LOUD BY ANYBODY IN THIS BAND AND IS RESERVED.**
12. **AND THE THINGS OF THIS BAND THAT ARE NOT DOCUMENTS:** boards off a window; a rule that a person may be entered by any person of the ward who can say the name, which is a rule with no fee and therefore no keeper and no date; nine doors' worth of chalk in a woman's pocket; one line on the back of a candle bill, which is not a question.

### 4. THE CLOCKS, AND THE ONE THAT DOES NOT RUN HERE

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The seventy-second week is 224 to 230 and the seventy-third is 231 to 237 and the seventy-fourth is 238 to 244. CHAPTER 350 IS SHELF DAY 225 AND CHAPTER 351 IS 226 AND CHAPTER 360 IS 235 AND CHAPTER 400 IS 275. HIS SHELF DAY IS HIS CHAPTER NUMBER LESS ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE, AND HIS MORNING COUNT IS HIS CHAPTER NUMBER LESS TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY, AND BOTH ARE ARITHMETIC AND NOT A CONVENTION. NO CHAPTER MAY MENTION THE TURN OF THE YEAR.**

**The bells: four a day, the first, the change, the noon, and the last, and the same four here as in the March.** The city also keeps the hour, and the hours are counted from the first light, and a person of a place says the hour and not the bell.

**THE MAR'S WEIGHTS DO NOT RUN HERE AND MAY NOT BE USED HERE.** Four bells a day, a stranger is three, a market is four, a boundary is seven, a debt called in is two, a fire is two, a burial is one — **and a weight is a weight in the county the plank is in, and Orison is not in that county, and a day in this city carries no weight at all because it is on no plank. A chapter of Volume 08 that adds a March weight to a day in Orison, or prints the plank's figure, has committed the defect the Volume 07 close spent eight sections on, in a place where nothing can check it.**

### 5. THE MONEY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 360, AND IT IS SIX FIGURES AND TWO UNITS

| Figure | Where it stands |
|---|---|
| **A Bench date, bought = 282d = £1 3s 6d** | **NAMED IN THE FICTION FOR THE FIRST TIME IN EIGHT VOLUMES, `357`, with 240 + 36 + 6 in the sentence and eighteen times fifteen and twelve against a carter's day.** |
| **A plate = 4s the hundred names = 48d; 200 names = 8s; 300 names = 12s = 144d** | `356`, `357`, and the product is in `357` with the working |
| **A keeper of a roll = 4s a year = 48d; the difference from a bell keeper's 1,260d is 1,212d = £5 1s** | `355`, `357`, `358` |
| **A round of the ward = 30 doors a fortnight on 900 doors = 420 days; a year is 364; the difference is 56** | `355`, with every factor in the sentence |
| **A carter = 18d a day; a copy = 3d, a penny a page** | CANON units, used as units |
| **The four wards are short 300 names; the plates cost 12s; a date costs 282d; the difference is 138d** | `357`, and the ward-by-ward figures are 200, 70 and 30 and the fourth ward is refused a figure |
| **The four hundred and eighty-five pounds; the levy at a bar; the two numbers on the market's slip** | **at ZERO in this band and spent** |

### 6. EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **The order's sixth line is empty and the word beside it is standing, and a rule that cannot be ended is being written in a city by one person, and the person is right about why, and the person is the only one who can write it.**
2. **A person is inside the protection when a roll has the name in it, and the roll says who answers a door, and about three hundred people in four wards do not answer a door and are outside a protection a magistrate put there on purpose.**
3. **Four records that have to agree and three of them do not, and the whole cost of the disagreement is eight shillings in one ward, and no form in this city says a man may call a meeting of a ward.**
4. **A woman of forty-four is the only person in this city anybody can ask about the roll, and she did not agree to it, she was not asked, she is not going to be thanked, and there is no form that will take a name back out of a record.**
5. **His own name is on one sheet in this city, put there by the Bench's own clerk under a written request for a witness, and there is no form to take it off, and he has said so out loud and been refused.**
6. **The number of beds free in six halls only goes up, and about half the beds in this city are empty, and a woman of fifty-eight on a stool has been chalking a number she can stand behind for five days and she knows the other one.**
7. **Nobody can be discharged from a hall, and the discharge is a document, and no office in this city issues one, and the reason is that the order's sixth line is empty.**
8. **The fourteenth is a date in a chamber upstairs, and the question on the slate is what the Bench is to do with a document it did not make, and a witness has been asked for, and the man of fifty-two at the rail and the plate-cutter are the other name or the other candidate, and nobody has asked either of them.**
9. **A map arrived in this city and says there is a thing under it that is not found, and nobody has opened anything, and nobody in this city knows who laid it or when or why, and the word the map uses has not been said out loud by anybody.**
10. **A woman of twenty-eight is going to about nine hundred doors on her own account with nine days of chalk and no rota and a promise, and he has promised, and a promise from him has cost a woman at a bar a thing she cannot put down, and **the promise is one day old, made in a yard at the ninth hour of `359` and still standing at the last of the light of `360` — REPAIRED, this entry said four days old, which is the age of the meeting at the standpipe and not of the promise**.**
11. **A man of forty-four cannot come down nine steps and is not on a roll, and there is no paper in this city that would put him in one, and his boards are off the window and nobody in the street knows.**
12. **About a hundred and forty people are dead and they stay dead and nobody has counted them properly and the fever is neither explained nor contained and it is a fever.**
13. **The right of refusal is unrestored and nobody has one, the four words at the head of the plank in the March are unprinted, the column of a hundred and forty-one figures is unadded and unmentioned, the corridor stands four hundred miles off with a bell owed, the porch in the valley is gone with no copy, the bell in the March is unhung, the six places in the lane stand and the seventh does not come back, and the nine copies of the map are nine copies.**
14. **His leg is the leg and the carrying is off nothing and no count of days since it cleared is printed anywhere, and the year of his own memory went in Chapter 197 and is not paid again.**
15. **AND THE ONE THIS BAND ADDED, WHICH IS NOT A STORY FINDING: the count of verbatim repetitions in a band with no frame in it is a third of the lowest of the five bands of Volume 07, and the reason is the absence of the frame and not a discipline, and the sixteen instances of the numeric hedge are one in one thousand three hundred and fifty-three words **in twenty-one thousand six hundred and forty-five words as the chapters now stand, which is twenty-one thousand six hundred and fifty-five as they were delivered, the declared method including the ten heading lines — REPAIRED, this said one thousand three hundred and forty-five, which is the rate computed on a word count taken with the heading lines removed** and the sixteen are a person's estimates.** `state/current.md` §3.

### 7. THE SIX THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **That Orison is about ninety thousand people and that the figure is an estimate given by a narrator twice, at `351` and `352`, **REPAIRED, this entry said *once, at `351`*, and `about ninety thousand` is in both**, and that five later chapters were cut back to *this city* rather than repeat it, and that a band which repeats it has reintroduced the hedge this band spent a day cutting.**
2. **That there is no weight column in this volume and no plank figure, and that the map at `state/current.md` §1 has NONE in it ten times on purpose.**
3. **That the panel count is one, that it is at `360:75` to `360:78`, and that bold is zero in the other nine files; the band had five emphatic bold spans in four other chapters and took them out, and a later pass that finds bold in Chapter 352 is reading a file this band has already fixed.**
4. **That Fenna Rusk refused her name on the fifth morning, in Chapter 355, and that the whole of Chapter 360 depends on it, **REPAIRED, this said the second morning and Chapter 355 is his fifth morning in the city; Chapter 352 is his second and Fenna Rusk is not in it**, and that a repair which makes her introduce herself in Chapter 355 destroys the climax of the book.**
5. **That the two round numbers are Chapter 355 and Chapter 360 and that nobody notices either, and that the correct treatment is the one Chapter 340 got.**
6. **That the count of the people who know he cannot read is printed nowhere, and that `cannot read` is at zero over these ten chapters, and that the band got through a city of thirty clerks by asking for things to be read aloud and never saying why.**

## VOLUME 07 IS CLOSED — CHAPTERS 301–350, *The Choir of Nine Anchors*. **THE FACT BASE FOR EVERYTHING AFTER IT, WRITTEN FOR A WRITER WHO HAS READ NONE OF THE VOLUME. The block below it, `## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 341–350`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase. The live record with the map in both calendars, the board chain, the running-total column, the fifteen prohibitions with their evidence, the instruments with their patterns and the money with its working is the top block of `state/current.md` under `## VOLUME 07 IS CLOSED`, and it outranks this block. VOLUME 07 IS CLOSED.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS, IN SIX PARTS.** The ground as it stands on Shelf day 225. The six places and the seventh that is gone. **Every document that exists in this county and where each one is, which is the part a Volume 08 writer most needs and the part a state file is worst at.** The clocks. The money. And the one list of everything still standing, which is the list a writer should read and not this paragraph.

### 1. THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE SECOND DAY OF THE SEVENTY-SECOND WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it, and a market on the green one day in a fortnight that nobody rings anything for.** The lane runs from village to village and not by the ground, through eleven villages. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and there is one kitchen in it that anybody will let a stranger stand in, and it is the kitchen at the end of the lane, and there are always nine people in it and the ninth is the smith's wife and the tenth never comes.** Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks.

**The tenth village and the hamlet behind it, eleven miles down the lane and off it, and the valley behind a track two miles off the lane on the other side, are all in the March's own county, and this is settled and a later writer must not re-open it.** `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0308.md:13` puts a hundred and forty such days in a year **in this county** of a bar that stands eleven miles down that lane. **So a market, a stranger, a debt called in, a burial, a fire and a boundary in any of those places are all March weights and all of them go on the plank at Coldharrow, and a day carries one of each kind, and four bells is the one weight that does not belong to a county. THE ONLY PLACE OUTSIDE THE COUNTY IN THIS VOLUME IS THE MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE.** The tenth village is a place with a bar at the end of it and a smithy with a room over it; the hamlet is eleven houses and about forty people on a rise behind the last of them with a burying ground on the rise; the valley is about sixty people, a bridge with one rail on it, a track of two miles, and a gate nobody in forty years has thought was a gate.

**Ottily Roke, forty-one, keeps the bell and the board and has kept them twenty-five years, and the third hand in the chalk at the foot of the plank is not added to.** The figure has exactly one writer and two carriers and no third: she writes it at the second light, and it reaches a man on the lane either because he reads it himself at the foot of the ladder or because the man of about thirty-eight brings it about the second hour in a cart, and that man is four days up a road. **A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND AND MUST NOT ADD A THIRD CARRIER.**

### 2. THE SIX PLACES AND THE SIX PEOPLE, AS THEY STAND AFTER CHAPTER 350, AND THE SEVENTH

1. **THE BOUNDARY WALK. STANDING.** Four hundred yards of hedge in the third village, walked in nine days, eleven names on the back of a flour bill in a kitchen in Ashgill, nine of them come, and a twelfth in a stranger's hand that the man of about sixty-three does not know he wrote. **The nine mornings ran from his eighty-fifth morning to his ninety-third; the ninth was Chapter 343, and nine of eleven walked it in the last of the wet; and he walked it a tenth time on the ninety-fifth morning on a day that was not one of the nine, with a stranger, and nobody came out on purpose and no bill said so.** Nobody has read the bill aloud. Nobody knows the twelfth name is on it and he is not going to be told, and a hand was not crossed out.
2. **THE SCALES. STANDING, AND IT DID NOT STOP.** A set of beam scales on a stone plinth at the end of a main street in a market four days up the lane, a toll in a wooden box with a slot in it, and a share that pays for the standard of the load, the water at the end of Church Lane, the bridge, the wall in the churchyard and the cross. **The man of about seventy is called Wenlock, is about seventy, has stood on that stone about forty-four years, and was named once, in nine words, in a street, in Chapter 328. HE HAS NOT BEEN ASKED FOR ANYTHING SINCE, HE WAS NOT ASKED IN THE LAST TWENTY CHAPTERS, HE IS ENTITLED TO STOP, AND NOBODY IS GOING TO ASK HIM.** His daughter of about fifty-eight does the sums in her head and is six months gone with a child and has not been asked what the slip in her own back room is for. **The slip is one market season's count and the year's count is double it; both numbers are on the page at `324` and neither is taken out of the slip.**
3. **THE LINE. STANDING.** Nine diggers on a rise behind the last of eleven houses, and a rule written nowhere, and nobody who can say who dug the first hole. **The woman of thirty came eleven miles on her own legs to a yard behind a smithy at Coldharrow on the ninety-third morning because nobody sent for her, and she said a NAME out loud, and the man who reads things out said it back. She has said what she wants three times, the ninth word of the third time is *mine*, and she has not said it a fourth time and is not going to be thanked.**
4. **THE FUND. STANDING, AND NOBODY IS HOLDING IT.** A levy on loads at a bar in the tenth village, a column on a shelf in a room over a smithy, and a woman with a bag who carries the book nine times a year and is not going to be asked and is right. **Three pence a load, about nine loads a day, a hundred and forty days, fifteen pounds fifteen shillings a village a year, and it goes out in the same week, every penny, for about thirty-one years, to nobody who is a person in a record.** The man of thirty-four went up the lane on six mornings with no order in his head because the stranger refused to give him a list, and he got four of the six. **The man of about thirty-eight is four days up a road with a bag and a bad back and a book he has not decided about, and his own sentence arrived in a market street four days up the lane without his consent, and nobody has asked him whether he wants it carried, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles that will tell him.**
5. **THE MILE. STANDING.** Nine householders, ninety years, each a length, unpaid, and a man of about thirty with a cart and a beast who is the third of the nine. **A DEBT WAS CALLED IN AT THE FOURTH HOUSE ON THE MORNING OF THE CLIMAX, IT IS THE SECOND TIME, THE DOOR WAS SHUT, AND NOBODY RESOLVED IT. A BARROW GOES TO THAT GATE AT THE FIFTH HOUR AND NOBODY IN THAT LANE KNOWS. AND THE MAN OF THIRTY IS ON THE BELL NETWORK, WHICH IS THE THING HE SPENT A MONTH SAYING WOULD HAPPEN, AND HE WANTS OFF IT AND IS NOT GOING TO BE.**
6. **THE PERSON. GONE, PHYSICALLY, AND IT CANNOT BE GOT BACK.** A board in a porch with eleven names on it, undated, no column, no heading, no mark against any of the eleven, in a valley of about sixty people two miles off a track off the lane. **It went out of that porch between the fourth hour and the last of the light on his ninety-eighth morning, and there is no copy of it in four hundred miles and there is not going to be one, because the arrangement was that one woman went down four mornings a year and there was never any writing about it. SHE IS SEVENTY-FOUR AND SHE IS ENTITLED TO STOP AND SHE STOPPED AND NOBODY IS TO BLAME AND NOBODY IS RELIEVED AND NOBODY WAS THANKED.**
7. **THE SEVENTH, WHICH IS THE PORCH, WHICH IS THE BRIAR MARCH POSITION, WHICH IS PHYSICALLY BURNED.** The arrangement nobody in the volume knew about is a hearth in that porch with a box in it and a flue through the roof and ash in the box from four hundred mornings, and one woman of seventy-four laying a fire in it on cold mornings for about forty years and going down four mornings a year and telling nobody what for. **She did not go down on the morning it burned, because she is seventy-four and it was cold and she has a stick. NOT ONE PERSON IN THAT VALLEY KNEW THERE WAS A FIRE IN THERE UNTIL AN HOUR AGO, INCLUDING HIM.**
8. **THE EIGHTH AND THE NINTH STAY MISSING.** The map says the two words *not found* and a place — the basin, and the town. Nobody in this county has been to either, nobody is going to this month, nobody knows who laid them or when or why, no character in any of the fifty chapters said it, and no panel named it.
9. **AND THE BELL NETWORK THAT IS THE REPLACEMENT, AND IT IS NOT A REPAIR.** A hundred and forty people in eleven villages, something that can be struck, by anybody who happens to be near it, and no keeper. A goat's bell on a gate bar, a handbell, two harness bells off a dead cart, a hoe handle against a wall. Nine weeks, said twice by a woman of about twenty-nine who counted it. **No figure is attached to any of it, no wage is being paid, nobody is buying the wage in the tower at Coldharrow, which is still unhung and still owed, and the word *network* is used once in fifty chapters.**

**AND THE RECORD, WHICH IS STILL A PERSON IN IT AND CANNOT BE LIFTED OUT.** A woman of about thirty-four at a bar at the end of the tenth village, twenty-two years, has been a person in a record since the eighty-eighth morning, because a stranger with a bad leg went eleven miles and said the price out loud before he asked. **She said no to nothing, she was not offered anything she could refuse, she was not asked, she was not thanked, she is not going to be, and there is no instrument in this country or in any county four hundred miles off that will lift her out of it.** In the last twenty chapters she is in no chapter at all and he stands at a bar in that village in Chapter 346 and looks at her and she looks at him and neither of them says one word about any of it.

### 3. EVERY DOCUMENT THAT EXISTS IN THIS COUNTY, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS — **AND THIS IS THE LIST A VOLUME 08 WRITER MOST NEEDS AND THE ONE A STATE FILE IS WORST AT**

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** A plank on the north wall of the tower, at the second light, in words, with a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and a third hand in the chalk at the foot that is forty-nine days old at the start of this volume and has not been added to. **64,154 is the last morning of this volume and no chapter prints it; 64,162 is Chapter 351's morning and is printed nowhere.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it, and a man of forty called Corvin Hale who signed it out loud with the working printed and is alive in his own field and is owed a bell nobody bought. **Named three times in Volume 07, at `306:65`, `336:5` and `343:21`, and never as a thing that is open.**
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines, copied and sealed at Sallowby, under the seat of a timber cart. **Not mentioned.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in a woman's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow. **Not mentioned.**
5. **FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **Not opened, not refused, not mentioned.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year and not copied and not shown, a question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered, and a third place he can see and has never been able to make out. **He said in Chapter 341 that he would read it out again on any morning to anybody and that nobody in eleven villages has ever asked him, and nobody asked him, and he was not thanked and did not want to be. In Chapter 344 he told a room that there are seven and would not say who, and the protagonist refused to go and look. Both of his unspent things are still unspent. NO COUNT OF THE QUESTION IS PRINTED IN ANY FILE AND NONE MAY BE.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures. **Not mentioned.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned and not summed and it may not be summed. It is described in its FORM four times, all four in Chapter 301, and nobody ever added it up and it was refused twice out loud on purpose, and no chapter in fifty says what the figures count.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned, and it gained no place and lost no place in fifty chapters.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Named once, at `343:21`, in a sentence about what was produced to a room, and not as a thing that is open.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed. **Not mentioned.**
12. **THE MARKET'S FOOLSCAP BOOK AND THE SLIP IN IT.** One book, one slip, on a shelf in a back room at the end of the main street of a market town four days up the lane, in a house that has had it a hundred and forty years, and the market's own book is a second count in a second county and is sixty years old and nobody in that town knows what the year is. **THE SLIP SAYS FOUR THOUSAND EIGHT HUNDRED AND THE YEAR IS NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED, and both numbers are named on the page at `324:71` and `324:75` and read aloud off the slip by the man who keeps the plinth, and NEITHER IS TAKEN OUT OF IT. IN THE LAST TWENTY CHAPTERS THE SLIP IS THE WORD, TWICE, AND GIVES NO FIGURE.**
13. **THE SIX UNSIGNED PIECES OF PAPER, ON THE BACKS OF FLOUR BILLS, GONE OUT OF A ROOM OVER A SMITHY IN SIX HANDS, AND NOBODY KNOWS WHERE ALL SIX ARE AND NOBODY PUT A NAME ON ONE.** They are the round of turns: each one says whose turn comes next. **The seventh of them is a list of who holds the nine copies of the map, and it has a name on it that the person did not put there.**
14. **THE PARTIAL MAP. NINE THINGS ON A BOARD THE SIZE OF A DOOR, ON THE BACK OF A FLOUR MILL'S SACK, IN NINE HANDS, IN NINE PLACES, IN ABOUT NINE WEEKS, IN A MARKET. NINE COPIES EXIST. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HOLD ONE. NOBODY HOLDS THE INVENTORY AND THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS COUNTRY THAT SAYS A MAN MAY APPOINT ANYBODY TO KNOW. Six standing, one gone, two not found. He is on it nowhere and there is not going to be a name at the foot of it, and a hand is not crossed out on the flour bill.**
15. **AND THE THINGS THE VOLUME PUT IN THE WORLD THAT ARE NOT DOCUMENTS:** a want in a boy of seventeen's mouth about being asked to read a book out; a want in a man of sixty-three's mouth about who comes after him; a want in a man with a cart's mouth about being taken off a list; a want in a woman who cannot write's mouth about being taught; a want in a woman of twenty-nine's mouth about a name somebody put at the foot of a board; a hearth and a box in a porch that nobody knew was there; a hundred and forty somethings that can be struck, built in nine weeks; and four people who carried a sentence to a market town without knowing they were carrying it.

### 4. THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THE VOLUME DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week, so Chapters 1 to 350 run Shelf day one to Shelf day 225 and the five weeks Volume 07 crossed are the sixty-fifth to the seventy-second. **Chapter 251 is the first morning of this man in this country and Chapter 301 is the fifty-first and Chapter 350 is the hundredth and Chapter 351 is the hundred and first. NO CHAPTER OF THE FIFTY MENTIONS THE TURN OF THE YEAR.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed. THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS IS SETTLED: four bells a day; a stranger is three; a market is four; a boundary is seven; a debt called in is two; a fire is two; a burial is one. THE ONLY NON-ORDINARY DAYS IN THE FIFTY ARE THE ELEVEN AT `335` AND `345`, WHICH ARE FOUR BELLS AND A BOUNDARY, AND THE TWELVE AT `348`, WHICH IS FOUR BELLS AND A MARKET AND A DEBT CALLED IN AND A FIRE AND IS THE FIRST DAY IN FIFTY CHAPTERS THAT CARRIED FOUR OF THE SEVEN.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is not added to.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this volume.**

### 5. THE MONEY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 350, AND IT IS FOUR TABLE FIGURES AND TWO UNITS

| Figure | Where it stands |
|---|---|
| **A keeper's year = 9d × 140 = 1,260d = £5 5s** | **NAMED, WITH THE WORKING, at `303:81` and `303:95`.** |
| **A bell, hung, with rope, and the difference of one pound ten** | **NAMED, WITH THE WORKING, at `303:77`, `303:91` and `303:95`, and the chapter prints a wrong version first and a man corrects it in a room: 31 × 48d = 1,488; +132d = 1,620d = £6 15s; 1,620 − 1,260 = 360d = £1 10s. THE BELL IN THE COLDHARROW TOWER WAS NOT RUNG ON ANY MORNING IN THE FIFTY CHAPTERS, A DAY NOTHING IS ON ATTACHES NO FIGURE TO IT, NOBODY IS BUYING THE WAGE, AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** |
| **The levy at a bar = 3d × 9 × 140 = 3,780d = £15 15s** | **NAMED, WITH THE WORKING, at `308:17`, and again at `308:19`, `313:15` and `322:31`, and NOT NAMED IN THE LAST TWENTY CHAPTERS AT ALL.** |
| **The four hundred and eighty-five pounds** | **NOT NAMED, AT ZERO, IN ALL FIFTY CHAPTERS. THE THREAD IS SPENT.** |
| **A copy** | **A UNIT, NAMED TWICE, at `319:25` and `344:51`: threepence at a scrivener over a chandler, and a penny a page. NO PRODUCT ANYWHERE AND *ABOUT NINETY DEEDS A YEAR* IS AT ZERO.** |
| **A cart** | **A UNIT: eighteen pence a day, at `309:13` and five other lines. THE CARTER'S YEAR IS NOT NAMED AND IS NOT MULTIPLIED.** |

| **The difference, if it were made up out of the poor list** | **NOT PRICED. About ninety names on a list at a cross in nine towns, and no product anywhere, and the woman of about sixty-one at the cross is not asked to choose.** |

### 6. EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **A corridor is standing in a valley of about a hundred and forty people four hundred miles off and is not going to be torn up. A man of forty called Corvin Hale signed it out loud with the working printed, is alive in his own field, and is owed a bell nobody bought.** Named three times in this volume and never undone.
2. **A status is on the inside of a door in a village of about ninety people and it has no gate, no toll, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, and about four people in a room understood what that meant and about nine did not.** Named once.
3. **Four people in two counties can be asked about something, twice as many as there were fifty days ago, and one of them is about sixty and there is nobody coming, and one of them cannot write and shut her gate bar for four seconds because a hen was out.** They were not counted in this volume and are not to be counted in any file.
4. **A word is not a person, and the instrument for turning a word into a person is a name at the foot of a thing. AND THE VOLUME ADDED THE THIRD HALF OF THAT FINDING: a name a man of thirty-four copied onto a list of who holds nine copies, without being asked and without knowing, is not a name in a place either, and the person it was put there finds out about it in front of nine people and there is no form in this country that says a man may remove a name from a piece of paper once it is on there.** `350`
5. **A column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and may not be summed, and what the figures count is not known to anybody.** Described in its form four times and not mentioned again.
6. **A right of refusal that is a permission with no second half is unrestored and nobody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the fifty chapters of this volume.**
7. **A bell in the March is unhung and a bell in Draymoor is owed and is not rung, and the difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten, and the difference was said out loud in a kitchen in Ashgill in Chapter 303 and not said since. AND THE BELL NETWORK THAT IS THE PUBLIC REPLACEMENT FOR THE BURNED SEVENTH IS NOT A BELL IN A TOWER, WAS NOT HUNG, AND IS NOT GOING TO BE.**
8. **Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead and was named twice, at `332:45` and `332:53`, and is not named again. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended.**
9. **The carrying is off nothing. The leg is the leg. No chapter in this volume prints a count of days since the leg cleared, and the leg is used as a consequence and as the reason he cannot be in two places, in most of the fifty.**
10. **The Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half in this volume and which is not named in any of the fifty chapters.**
11. **The question what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network is asked in its stated form NOWHERE, and all three shapes it took in Volume 06 are spent, and `outline/volume-07.md:104` forbids a fourth.**
12. **WHY THE MISSING POSITIONS ANSWER IN A VOICE THAT SOUNDS LIKE THE SYSTEM WAS ASKED ONCE, NEAR THE END, BY A WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FOUR WITH A BARROW OF APPLES WHO HAD NO STAKE IN IT, IN THE PLAINEST WORDS A PERSON IN THIS COUNTRY HAS: THE BASIN AND THE TOWN. IS THERE SOMEBODY DOWN THERE WITH THEM. IT IS NOT ANSWERED. NOBODY IN THAT MARKET ANSWERED HER AND NOTHING ANSWERED HER AND NO BOARD SAID ANYTHING AND NOTHING IN THAT VALLEY AND NOTHING UNDER THAT BASIN AND NOTHING UNDER THAT TOWN SAID ANYTHING. THE SYSTEM PUT NOTHING UP, RENDERED NOTHING, REPLIED TO NOTHING, AND SAID NOTHING, AND THE MAN OF THIRTY-ONE DID NOT ANSWER HER BECAUSE THE ONLY INSTRUMENT HE HAS IS A QUESTION ASKED IN FRONT OF OTHER PEOPLE AND HE HAS USED IT TWICE IN A MONTH AND BOTH TIMES IT MADE SOMEBODY A PERSON IN A RECORD.** `350`
13. **THE MIDPOINT IS SPENT AND NOTHING ABOUT IT MAY BE SPENT AGAIN.** The arrangement did not stop, the untruth is the paper and is sixty years old, the honest version is four sentences, about ninety people cannot pay it, the man of about seventy is entitled to stop, and neither number is to be taken out of the slip.
14. **THE HEARING WAS HELD AND IT WAS NOT LINKED.** It was held in a yard behind a smithy with nine people in it and no paper, on the ninety-third morning, and four of the six turns were said in four places on the ninety-eighth morning, and the fifth could not be said and stopped the round, and the sixth and the seventh are in a porch and were not said, and nobody said the sixth or the seventh because nobody has asked the woman of seventy-four anything about anything. `343`, `348`
15. **A BURDEN CARRIED BY TWO PEOPLE IS A BURDEN NEITHER OF THEM CAN PUT DOWN ALONE, and the person is a woman of about thirty-four at a bar, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles, and he has been looking for one for a hundred mornings, and she said it at a cross in the open at about the second hour on the ninetieth morning and it was in a market street four days up the lane a day and a half later and he was four days of road from it.** `338`, `340`, `342`
16. **Draymoor is not one of the six and he did not go there in any of the five bands, and the word Draymoor is at zero across Chapters 301 to 350, and the finding that belongs to it was said in one paragraph in Chapter 306 and rests there.**
17. **AND THE SIX PLACES ARE FIXED AND THE SELF-ARRIVED CLASS IS ZERO AND IT DID NOT WIDEN, AND NOBODY WAS SENT FOR ANYWHERE IN THE FIFTY CHAPTERS, WITH ONE EXCEPTION THAT IS RECORDED AS AN EXCEPTION: a smith's wife sent for a man of fifty-two at the bar at about the ninth hour, which the chapter says is the only time anybody has sent for anybody in that room in nine years, and he had not been asked to be there and did not say so and did not offer, and he said nine words and went back to work.** `344:89`
18. **AND THE ONE FINDING NO CHAPTER MAKES AND NO FILE RESOLVES: in Chapters 301 to 310 the figure on the plank does not carry forward from the day the same chapter describes, in three places that four bells alone proves, and in Chapters 311 to 350 it carries forward in every one of forty rows. It is not repaired here because a close is not a repair phase, and Chapter 351's morning is unaffected by it.** `state/current.md` §2
19. **AND THE ONE CANDIDATE CONTRADICTION THE CLOSE FOUND AND DID NOT RESOLVE, WITH ITS ARITHMETIC CORRECTED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR: two passages give the same man of about seventy-four in the same hamlet two different *since* dates, at `317:15` and `336:49`, and the two dates are **FOURTEEN days apart — the last day of the sixty-sixth week is Shelf day 188 and the last day of the sixty-eighth is Shelf day 202, and 202 − 188 = 14 — where the close printed one hundred and forty-one. The old man is *in his own house* and not in the second house; the second house in that hamlet is the man of about fifty-one's, at `336:43`.** The volume does not say whether there are two old men in one house. Full working, and both passages quoted, at `state/current.md` §3.9.

### 7. THE SEVEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **Chapter 351's morning is 64,162 and Chapter 350's is 64,154, and the reason is that Chapter 348's own day is twelve and not ten because the burned porch is a fire in this county.** A figure carried by a hand-off with a false working has been this project's defect class seven times and this is the seventh.
2. **The board chain holds from Chapter 311 and does not hold in Chapters 301 to 310, and the running-total column is the only instrument that finds it.** Do not inherit the Batch 0001 ring line and do not add the rings together and call the result a morning.
3. **The span scan is a span scan, the method is declared whole at `state/current.md` §3.1, and **it does not reproduce thirteen published numbers and the close's claim that it did is withdrawn**: the method pools the ten chapters of a band into one tally before it counts, and run per chapter — as the close first declared it — it returns 187 over the volume and not the figure printed under it. Measured, the five bands are 235 / 144 / 163 / 122 / **534** at seventy characters and **877 / 797 / 1,353 / 1,201 / 1,838** at forty, and the whole-volume pass is 3,836 and 12,867 and is not the sum of the bands. Bands one to four at seventy characters reproduce their published figures exactly and no band reproduces at forty. The fifth is the highest and not a fall.**
4. **The word *nine* in this project's record is a substring count, not a word count. The word count over Volume 07 is 777 and the substring count is 906, and the number that has been printed about something else for two phases is also 777, and that is a coincidence.**
5. **The numeric hedge is 552 and the undeclared time-of-day clock is 184 and the beat tic `it took` is 66, and no one of the three includes another, and the volume's real arithmetic is all three.**
6. **The count of people who know he cannot read is printed nowhere, and the string *cannot read* is at zero over Chapters 301 to 350, and the phrase *man of thirty-one* went to zero in the last band and that is a change in the prose and not only in a count.**
7. **The money is not absent from this volume. It is in Chapters 303, 308 and 324 with the working printed in the sentence, and a close or a batch that reads the last band's block instead of the chapters will say the volume printed no figure, which this close did on its own first pass and had to correct.**

## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 341–350, *The Hearing, The Seventh, And The Map*. **THE FACT BASE FOR EVERYTHING AFTER IT. The block below it, `## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 331–340, *Delegation*`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase. The live record with the map in both calendars, the board chain, the fifteen prohibitions with their file and line, the instruments with their patterns and the money is the top block of `state/current.md` under `## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005 IS DELIVERED`, and it outranks this block. VOLUME 07 IS CLOSED.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the state of the Briar March, of a road eleven miles off a lane, of a valley off a track, of a market town four days up the lane, and of one person in a record, at the end of Chapter 350, which is his hundredth morning, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the ten chapters. Every figure was measured against the ten chapter files after the prose was written. TWO FIGURES IN IT ARE CORRECTIONS TO A HAND-OFF AND NOT TO A CHAPTER AND ARE MARKED.**

### THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE SECOND DAY OF THE SEVENTY-SECOND WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it, and a market on the green one day in a fortnight that nobody rings anything for.** The lane runs from village to village and not from north to south, through eleven villages. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and there is one kitchen in it that anybody will let a stranger stand in, and it is the kitchen at the end of the lane, and there are always nine people in it and the ninth is the smith's wife and the tenth never comes.** Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks.

**THE TENTH VILLAGE AND THE HAMLET BEHIND IT, ELEVEN MILES DOWN THE LANE AND OFF IT, AND THE VALLEY BEHIND A TRACK TWO MILES OFF THE LANE ON THE OTHER SIDE, ARE ALL IN THE MARCH'S OWN COUNTY, WHICH IS A FACT THIS VOLUME SETTLED AND A LATER WRITER MUST NOT RE-OPEN.** `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0308.md:13` puts a hundred and forty such days in a year **in this county** of a bar that stands eleven miles down that lane. **SO A MARKET, A STRANGER, A DEBT CALLED IN, A BURIAL, A FIRE AND A BOUNDARY IN ELEVEN VILLAGES ARE ALL MARCH WEIGHTS AND ALL OF THEM GO ON THE PLANK AT COLDHARROW, AND THIS IS THE REASON THE DAY OF CHAPTER 348 IS TWELVE AND NOT TEN, AND THE REASON A PORCH THAT BURNS IN A VALLEY OFF THIS LANE IS ON A BOARD NINE MILES AWAY.** The only place outside the county in this volume is the market town four days up the lane. The tenth village is a place with a bar at the end of it and a smithy with a room over it; the hamlet is eleven houses and about forty people on a rise behind the last of them with a burying ground on the rise; the valley is about sixty people, a bridge with one rail on it, a track of two miles, and a gate that nobody in forty years has thought was a gate.

### THE SIX PLACES AND THE SIX PEOPLE, AS THEY STAND AFTER CHAPTER 350, AND THE SEVENTH

1. **THE BOUNDARY WALK. RESTORED, in the only way this country restores anything, which is by a person turning up on a day.** Four hundred yards of hedge, walked in nine days, eleven names on the back of a flour bill in a kitchen, nine of them come, and a twelfth in a stranger's hand that the man of about sixty-three does not know he wrote. **The nine mornings ran from his eighty-fifth morning to his ninety-third; the ninth was Chapter 343 and it was walked in the last of the wet by nine of eleven; and he walked it a tenth time on the ninety-fifth morning on a day that was not one of the nine, with a stranger, and nobody came out on purpose and no bill said so.** Nobody has read the bill aloud. Nobody knows the twelfth name is on it and he is not going to be told.
2. **THE SCALES. UNCHANGED, and it did not stop, and it is not going to.** A set of beam scales on a stone plinth at the end of a main street in a market four days up the lane, a toll in a wooden box with a slot in it, a share that pays for the standard, the water at the end of Church Lane, the bridge, the wall in the churchyard and the cross, and about four hundred loads go over that bridge in a week of a season. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY HAS NOT BEEN ASKED FOR ANYTHING SINCE HE SAID NINE WORDS IN HIS OWN YARD, HE WAS NOT ASKED IN THIS BAND, HE IS NOT NAMED IN THIS BAND, HE WAS IN CHAPTER 342 WITH HIS HANDS BEHIND HIS BACK AND SAID NOTHING, AND HE IS ENTITLED TO STOP.** The one reference to the slip in this band is the word, twice, and gives no figure.
3. **THE LINE. UNCHANGED.** Nine diggers on a rise behind the last of eleven houses, and a rule written nowhere, and nobody who can say who dug the first hole. **The woman of thirty came eleven miles on her own legs to a yard behind a smithy at Coldharrow on the ninety-third morning because nobody sent for her, and she said a NAME there, and the man who reads things out said it back. She has said what she wants three times and the ninth word of the third time is *mine*, and she has not said it a fourth time and is not going to be thanked.**
4. **THE FUND. UNCHANGED, and nobody is holding it.** A levy on loads at a bar in the tenth village, a column on a shelf in a room over a smithy, and a woman with a bag who carries the book nine times a year and is not going to be asked and is right. **The man of thirty-four went up the lane on six mornings with no order in his head because the stranger refused to give him a list, and he got four of the six and has not got the other two. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT IS FOUR DAYS UP A ROAD WITH A BAG AND A BAD BACK AND A BOOK HE HAS NOT DECIDED ABOUT, AND HIS OWN SENTENCE ARRIVED IN A MARKET STREET FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE TWO MORNINGS AGO, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM WHETHER HE WANTS IT CARRIED, AND THERE IS NO INSTRUMENT IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES THAT WILL TELL HIM.**
5. **THE MILE. UNCHANGED, and a tenth person is still on it and still cannot say no.** Nine householders, ninety years, each a length, unpaid, and a man of about thirty with a cart and a beast who is the third of the nine. **A DEBT WAS CALLED IN AT THE FOURTH HOUSE ON THE MORNING OF CHAPTER 348, IT IS THE SECOND TIME, THE DOOR WAS SHUT, AND NOBODY RESOLVED IT AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE RESOLVED BY A STRANGER WITH A LEG. A BARROW GOES TO THAT GATE AT THE FIFTH HOUR AND NOBODY IN THAT LANE KNOWS AND THE WIDOW'S HIP IS WHAT IT IS FOR. AND THE MAN OF THIRTY IS ON THE BELL NETWORK, WHICH IS THE THING HE SPENT A MONTH SAYING WOULD HAPPEN, AND HE WANTS OFF IT AND IS NOT GOING TO BE.**
6. **THE PERSON. GONE, PHYSICALLY, AND IT CANNOT BE GOT BACK.** A board in a porch with eleven names on it, undated, no column, no heading, no mark against any of the eleven, in a valley of about sixty people two miles off a track off the lane. **IT WENT OUT OF THAT PORCH BETWEEN THE FOURTH HOUR AND THE LAST OF THE LIGHT ON HIS NINETY-EIGHTH MORNING, AND THERE IS NO COPY OF IT IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE, BECAUSE THE ARRANGEMENT WAS THAT ONE WOMAN WENT DOWN FOUR MORNINGS A YEAR AND THERE WAS NEVER ANY WRITING ABOUT IT. SHE IS SEVENTY-FOUR AND SHE IS ENTITLED TO STOP AND SHE STOPPED AND NOBODY IS TO BLAME AND NOBODY IS RELIEVED AND NOBODY WAS THANKED.** The finding that a thing kept alive by one person is a thing that stops when that person stops was said by her, in her own mouth, in a doorway, and not by a narrator.
7. **THE SEVENTH, WHICH IS THE PORCH, WHICH IS THE BRIAR MARCH POSITION, WHICH IS PHYSICALLY BURNED.** **The arrangement nobody in this volume knew about is a hearth in that porch with a box in it and a flue through the roof and ash in the box from four hundred mornings, and one woman of seventy-four laying a fire in it on the cold mornings for about forty years and going down four mornings a year and telling nobody what for, and nobody in four hundred miles knowing there was a fire in there because nobody has ever asked her what she goes down for.**
8. **THE EIGHTH AND THE NINTH STAY MISSING.** The map says the two words *not found* and a place. Nobody in this county has been to either, nobody is going to this month, nobody knows who laid them or when or why, and no character in any of the fifty chapters said it and no panel named it.
9. **AND THE BELL NETWORK THAT IS THE REPLACEMENT, AND IT IS NOT A REPAIR.** A hundred and forty people in eleven villages, something that can be struck, by anybody who happens to be near it, and no keeper. A goat's bell on a gate bar, a handbell, two harness bells off a dead cart, a hoe handle against a wall. Nine weeks, said twice by a woman of about twenty-nine who counted it. **NO FIGURE IS ATTACHED TO ANY OF IT AND NO WAGE IS BEING PAID AND NOBODY IS BUYING THE WAGE IN THE TOWER AT COLDHARROW, WHICH IS STILL UNHUNG AND STILL OWED.**

### THE DOCUMENTS THAT EXIST AFTER CHAPTER 350, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** Above. 64,154 is the last morning of this volume, though no chapter in this band prints a figure for it, two hands and a third in chalk, and the third has not been added to and nobody has told him to wipe it off. **THE FIGURE HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER AND TWO CARRIERS AND NO OTHERS: THE WRITER IS OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, AT THE SECOND LIGHT, AND HE GETS IT ONE OF TWO WAYS — he reads it himself off the wall at the foot of the ladder (341, 343, 348) or the man of about thirty-eight brings it about the second hour, and that man is four days up a road and does not appear in any of the ten. A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND AND MUST NOT ADD A THIRD CARRIER. HE WAS AT COLDHARROW AT THE SECOND LIGHT IN THREE OF THE TEN MORNINGS AND IN THE OTHER SEVEN HE WAS NOT AND EACH OF THOSE SEVEN SAYS WHY IN ITS OWN FIRST PARAGRAPH.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it. **Not mentioned in any of the fifty chapters of this volume and it is still standing.**
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines, copied and sealed at Sallowby, under the seat of a timber cart. **Not mentioned.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in a woman's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow. **Not mentioned in any of the fifty chapters of this volume.**
5. **FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **Not opened, not refused, not mentioned.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year, not copied and not shown. A question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered, and a third place he can see and has never been able to make out. **HE SAID IN CHAPTER 341 THAT HE WOULD READ IT OUT AGAIN ON ANY MORNING TO ANYBODY AND THAT NOBODY IN ELEVEN VILLAGES HAS EVER ASKED HIM, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM AND HE WAS NOT THANKED AND DID NOT WANT TO BE. IN CHAPTER 344 HE TOLD A ROOM THAT THERE ARE SEVEN AND WOULD NOT SAY WHO, AND THE PROTAGONIST REFUSED TO GO AND LOOK. BOTH OF HIS UNSPENT THINGS ARE STILL UNSPENT. NO COUNT OF THE QUESTION IS PRINTED IN ANY FILE.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures. **Not mentioned.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned and not summed and it may not be summed.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned and it gained no place and lost no place.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Not mentioned.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed. **Not mentioned.**
12. **THE MARKET'S FOOLSCAP BOOK AND THE SLIP IN IT.** One book, one slip, one number, on a shelf in a back room at the end of the main street of a market town four days up the lane. **The word is used twice in Chapters 341 to 350 and neither figure is given and neither is taken out of the slip. The thread is spent.**
13. **THE SIX UNSIGNED PIECES OF PAPER, ON THE BACKS OF FLOUR BILLS, GONE OUT OF A ROOM OVER A SMITHY IN SIX HANDS, AND NOBODY KNOWS WHERE ALL SIX ARE AND NOBODY PUT A NAME ON ONE.** The seventh of them is a list of who holds the nine copies of the map and a name on it that the person did not put there.
14. **THE PARTIAL MAP. NINE THINGS ON A BOARD THE SIZE OF A DOOR, ON THE BACK OF A FLOUR MILL'S SACK, IN NINE HANDS, IN NINE PLACES, IN ABOUT NINE WEEKS. NINE COPIES EXIST. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HOLD ONE. NOBODY HOLDS THE INVENTORY AND THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS COUNTRY THAT SAYS A MAN MAY APPOINT ANYBODY TO KNOW.** Six standing, one gone, two not found. **He is on it nowhere and there is not going to be a name at the foot of it.**
15. **AND THE THINGS THIS BAND PUT IN THE WORLD THAT ARE NOT DOCUMENTS:** a want in a boy of seventeen's mouth about being asked to read a book out; a want in a man of sixty-three's mouth about who comes after him; a want in a man with a cart's mouth about being taken off a list; a want in a woman who cannot write's mouth about being taught; a want in a woman of twenty-nine's mouth about a name somebody put at the foot of a board; a hearth and a box in a porch that nobody knew was there; and four people who carried a sentence to a market town without knowing they were carrying it.

### THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THIS BAND DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The seventieth week is 210 to 216 and the seventy-first is 217 to 223 and the seventy-second begins at 224. Batch 0005 ran Shelf days 216 to 225, the ninety-first morning to the hundredth. NOTHING TURNS THE GRAIN YEAR IN THIS BAND, Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a new grain year and it is a hundred and thirty-four days after the last morning of this band, and no chapter mentions the turn and no chapter mentions the year.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed. AND A WEIGHT IS A WEIGHT IN THE COUNTY THE PLANK IS IN AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THE TENTH VILLAGE, THE HAMLET AND THE VALLEY ARE IN THIS COUNTY AND THE MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE IS NOT. FOUR BELLS IS THE ONE WEIGHT THAT IS NOT PLACE-BOUND, AND A DAY CARRIES EACH KIND OF WEIGHT ONCE, SO A SECOND MARKET IN A SECOND COUNTY IS NOT A SECOND MARKET.** **THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS, UNCHANGED: four bells a day; a stranger is three; a market is four; a boundary is seven; a debt called in is two; a fire is two; a burial is one. A LATER WRITER SHOULD TREAT THE TABLE AS SETTLED. AND THE FIRST DAY IN FIFTY CHAPTERS THAT CARRIED FOUR OF THE SEVEN IS HIS NINETY-EIGHTH MORNING, WHICH IS FOUR BELLS AND A MARKET AND A DEBT CALLED IN AND A FIRE.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is not added to. The market's own book is a second count in a second county and it is sixty years old and the slip in it is one market season and nobody in that town knows what the year is.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this band.**

### THE MONEY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 350, AND IT IS TWO UNITS

| Figure | Where it stands |
|---|---|
| **A keeper's year** | NOT NAMED, with the working or without it. The word *keeper* appears once, in Chapter 349, and it is a noun about a person: *And no keeper. That is the whole of the design.* |
| **A carter's year** | NOT NAMED. |
| **A bell, hung, with rope, and the difference of one pound ten** | NOT NAMED, AND NOT PRICED. **A BELL IN THE COLDHARROW TOWER WAS NOT RUNG ON A MARKET MORNING AND A DAY NOTHING IS ON AND NO FIGURE IS ATTACHED TO IT, AND NOBODY IS BUYING THE WAGE AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** |
| **The levy at a bar** | NOT NAMED WITH A FIGURE. The word is used once, in Chapter 344, with no unit, no count and no product. |
