### EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **A corridor is standing in a valley of about a hundred and forty people four hundred miles off and is not going to be torn up. A man of forty called Corvin Hale signed it out loud with the working printed, is alive in his own field, and is owed a bell nobody bought.** Not mentioned in any of the fifty chapters of this volume. Not undone.
2. **A status is on the inside of a door in a village of about ninety people and it has no gate, no toll, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, and about four people in a room understood what that meant and about nine did not.** Not mentioned.
3. **Four people in two counties can be asked about something, twice as many as there were fifty days ago, and one of them is about sixty and there is nobody coming, and one of them cannot write and shut her gate bar for four seconds because a hen was out.** They were not counted in this band and are not to be counted in any file.
4. **A word is not a person, and the instrument for turning a word into a person is a name at the foot of a thing. AND THE BAND ADDED THE THIRD HALF OF THAT FINDING: a name a man of thirty-four copied onto a list of who holds nine copies, without being asked and without knowing, is not a name in a place either, and the person it was put there finds out about it in front of nine people and there is no form in this country that says a man may remove a name from a piece of paper once it is on there.** `350`
5. **A column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and may not be summed, and what the figures count is not known to anybody.** Not mentioned in this volume.
6. **A right of refusal that is a permission with no second half is unrestored and nobody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the fifty chapters of this volume.**
7. **A bell in the March is unhung and a bell in Draymoor is owed and is not rung, and the difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten, and the difference was said out loud in a kitchen in Ashgill in Chapter 303 and has not been said since and was not said in this band. AND THE BELL NETWORK THAT IS THE PUBLIC REPLACEMENT FOR THE BURNED SEVENTH IS NOT A BELL IN A TOWER AND WAS NOT HUNG IN THIS BAND AND IS NOT GOING TO BE.**
8. **Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead and is not named again. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended.**
9. **The carrying is off nothing. The leg is the leg. No chapter in this band prints a count of days since the leg cleared, and the leg is used as a consequence in nine of the ten chapters and as the reason he cannot be in two places in eight of them.**
10. **The Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half in this band and which is not named in any of the ten chapters.**
11. **The question what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network is asked in its stated form NOWHERE, and all three shapes it took in Volume 06 are spent, and `outline/volume-07.md:104` forbids a fourth.**
12. **WHY THE MISSING POSITIONS ANSWER IN A VOICE THAT SOUNDS LIKE THE SYSTEM WAS ASKED ONCE, NEAR THE END OF THIS VOLUME, BY A WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FOUR WITH A BARROW OF APPLES WHO HAD NO STAKE IN IT, IN THE PLAINEST WORDS A PERSON IN THIS COUNTRY HAS: *THE BASIN AND THE TOWN. IS THERE SOMEBODY DOWN THERE WITH THEM.* IT IS NOT ANSWERED. NOBODY IN THAT MARKET ANSWERED HER AND NOTHING ANSWERED HER AND NO BOARD SAID ANYTHING AND NOTHING IN THAT VALLEY AND NOTHING UNDER THAT BASIN AND NOTHING UNDER THAT TOWN SAID ANYTHING. THE SYSTEM PUT NOTHING UP, RENDERED NOTHING, REPLIED TO NOTHING, AND SAID NOTHING, AND THE MAN OF THIRTY-ONE DID NOT ANSWER HER BECAUSE THE ONLY INSTRUMENT HE HAS IS A QUESTION ASKED IN FRONT OF OTHER PEOPLE AND HE HAS USED IT TWICE IN A MONTH AND BOTH TIMES IT MADE SOMEBODY A PERSON IN A RECORD.** `350`
13. **THE MIDPOINT IS SPENT AND NOTHING ABOUT IT MAY BE SPENT AGAIN.** The arrangement did not stop, the untruth is the paper, the honest version is four sentences, about ninety people cannot pay it, the man of about seventy is entitled to stop, and the name of the slip's season is not to be taken out of the slip.
14. **THE HEARING WAS HELD AND IT WAS NOT LINKED. It was held in a yard behind a smithy with nine people in it and no paper, on the ninety-third morning, and four of the six turns were said in four places on the ninety-eighth morning, and the fifth could not be said and stopped the round, and the sixth and the seventh are in a porch and were not said, and nobody said the sixth or the seventh because nobody has asked the woman of seventy-four anything about anything.** `343`, `348`
15. **A BURDEN CARRIED BY TWO PEOPLE IS A BURDEN NEITHER OF THEM CAN PUT DOWN ALONE, and the person is a woman of about thirty-four at a bar, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles, and he has been looking for one for a hundred mornings, and she said it at a cross in the open at about the second hour on the ninetieth morning and it was in a market street four days up the lane on the ninety-second morning and he was four days of road from it.** `338`, `340`, `342`
16. **Draymoor is not one of the six and he did not go there in any of the five bands, and the finding that belongs to it was said in one paragraph in Chapter 306 and rests there.**
17. **AND THE SIX PLACES ARE FIXED AND THE SELF-ARRIVED CLASS IS ZERO AND IT DID NOT WIDEN, AND NOBODY WAS SENT FOR ANYWHERE IN THIS BAND, WITH ONE EXCEPTION THAT IS RECORDED AS AN EXCEPTION: a smith's wife sent for a man of fifty-two at the bar at about the ninth hour, which the chapter says is the only time anybody has sent for anybody in that room in nine years, and he had not been asked to be there and did not say so and did not offer, and he said nine words and went back to work.**

### THE SEVEN THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM, AND THE SEVENTH IS NEW AND IS ABOUT THIS PHASE RATHER THAN ABOUT A FIGURE

1. **The morning figure appears in THREE of the ten chapters and not in seven, and a reader who expects ten will think seven are missing. They are not missing. They are the carrier rule working, and each of the seven says why in its own words in its own first paragraph.**
2. **The chain, and the rule that the morning in row *N* is row *N-1* plus the OWN DAY OF CHAPTER *N-1*, and that a table with the ring on the wrong row is a defect this project has been bitten six times. AND THE RING AT CHAPTER 348 IS TWELVE AND NOT TEN, AND IF A LATER BAND TAKES TEN FROM THE BATCH 0005 HAND-OFF IT WILL BUILD A WHOLE VOLUME ON A BAD BASE.**
3. **The intervals in Chapters 342, 343 and 347 were all wrong in the first writing and all four were found by hand, and two of them were intervals between this band's own chapters, which is the newest and most breakable kind.**
4. **The numeric hedge is FORTY-EIGHT and not one hundred and ninety, and the method is the one declared at the top block of `state/current.md` §3.12, the per-chapter table is 1 / 5 / 3 / 6 / 5 / 4 / 4 / 6 / 5 / 9 against 21,681 words, one in every four hundred and fifty-one, and every one of the forty-eight is the age of a person. AND THE UNDECLARED TIME-OF-DAY PATTERN RETURNS FORTY AND NOBODY IS COUNTING IT.**
5. **THE SPAN SCAN FIGURES IN THIS BLOCK WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS FILE BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY FIGURE HERE THAT A LATER PASS WAS PLANNING TO RE-RUN.** It stood at twenty distinct at seventy characters, *the lowest in the project*, and 288 at forty. **The band had declared half a method and had reported figures the method could not produce, and the block contradicted itself in the same sentence by saying the offending sentence was printed at both ends of a chapter, which makes occurrences exceed distinct.** The method is now declared whole at `state/current.md` §3.7 — every window of exactly *k* characters at every position **within each chapter separately**, keeping **only the windows that occur more than once** — and it reproduces all eight of the earlier bands' published figures exactly. **The true figures for this band are 700 distinct and 1,489 occurrences at seventy characters as first written and 533 and 1,079 after the review repair cut the shared opening wording, and 2,069 and 4,903 at forty becoming 1,836 and 4,282.** Batch 0004 was 122 and 1,198. **A later pass must not read 533 as a fall from 122: that figure was measured in a shorter book, and the honest reading is a long tail of incidental two-instance overlap, not a device.**
6. **The count of people who know he cannot read is printed nowhere, and the string *cannot read* is at zero over Chapters 311 to 350, and the number of *man of thirty-one* is ZERO, and a later pass should be told that a phrase at zero is a change in the prose and not only in a count.**
7. **AND THE SEVENTH, WHICH IS THE ONE THIS BLOCK COULD NOT HAVE FORESEEN: THE NEXT PHASE IS NOT A BATCH. Volume 07 is closed at Chapter 350 and the phase that runs next is `workspace/volume-07/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, which writes this file's next block and does not write a chapter.** The Batch 0005 review found that the band had created a Volume 08 plan-and-write prompt instead, which would have run first and left this close behind it. **A Volume 08 writer therefore must not expect to be handed a fact base for a volume that has not been closed, and the day-count, the money, the six places and every figure in this block will be re-measured by the close against fifty chapter files and may move.** The one figure the close is told it may not move without checking the working twice is **Chapter 351's morning, which is 64,162 and not 64,160, and Chapter 350's, which is 64,154 and not 64,152, and the reason is that Chapter 348's own day is twelve and not ten because the burned porch is a fire in this county.**

## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 331–340, *Delegation*. **THE FACT BASE FOR EVERYTHING AFTER IT. The block below it, `## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 321–330, *The Midpoint, And It Is A False Witness*`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase. The live record with the map in both calendars, the board chain, the fifteen prohibitions with their file and line, the instruments with their patterns and the money is the top block of `state/current.md` under `## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0004 IS DELIVERED`, and it outranks this block.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the state of the Briar March, of a road eleven miles off a lane, of a valley off a track, of a market town four days up the lane, and of one person in a record, at the end of Chapter 340, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the ten chapters. Every figure was measured against the ten chapter files after the prose was written.**

### THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE SIXTH MORNING OF THE SEVENTIETH WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it, and a market on the green one day in a fortnight that nobody rings anything for.** The lane runs from village to village and not from north to south, through eleven villages. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and there is one kitchen in it that anybody will let a stranger stand in, and it is the kitchen at the end of the lane, and there are always nine people in it and the ninth is the smith's wife and the tenth never comes.** Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks.

**THE TENTH VILLAGE AND THE HAMLET BEHIND IT, ELEVEN MILES DOWN THE LANE AND OFF IT, AND THEY ARE IN THE MARCH'S OWN COUNTY, WHICH IS A FACT THIS BAND SETTLED AND A LATER WRITER MUST NOT RE-OPEN.** `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0308.md:13` puts a hundred and forty such days in a year **in this county** of a bar that stands eleven miles down that lane. **SO A MARKET, A STRANGER, A DEBT CALLED IN, A BURIAL, A FIRE AND A BOUNDARY IN ELEVEN VILLAGES ARE ALL MARCH WEIGHTS AND ALL OF THEM GO ON THE PLANK AT COLDHARROW.** The only place outside the county in this volume is the market town four days up the lane. The tenth village is a place with a bar at the end of it and a smithy with a room over it; the hamlet is eleven houses and about forty people on a rise behind the last of them with a burying ground on the rise, and it is at the far end of the same road.

### THE SIX PLACES AND THE SIX PEOPLE, AS THEY STAND AFTER CHAPTER 340, UNCHANGED AND FIXED

1. **THE BOUNDARY WALK.** Four hundred yards of hedge in the third village up the lane, walked in nine days, eleven names on the back of a flour bill in a kitchen, nine of them come, and a twelfth in a stranger's hand that the man of about sixty-three does not know he wrote. **The nine mornings ran from the first morning of the seventieth week to the ninth, and the man of thirty-one is at a bridge on the fifth of them and was in a kitchen on the last morning of the sixty-ninth week knowing it and doing nothing about it.** Nine of eleven walked it on the first morning. Nobody has read the bill aloud. Nobody knows the twelfth name is on it.
2. **THE SCALES.** A set of beam scales on a stone plinth at the end of a main street in a market four days up the lane, a toll in a wooden box with a slot in it, a share that pays for the standard on the plinth, the water at the end of Church Lane, the bridge, the wall in the churchyard and the cross, and a number on a slip in a foolscap book in a back room that has been one market season for sixty years. **THE TOLL IS STILL BEING TAKEN, THE BEAM IS STILL ON THE PLINTH, ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED LOADS GO OVER IN A WEEK OF A SEASON, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY HAS NOT BEEN ASKED FOR ANYTHING SINCE HE SAID NINE WORDS IN HIS OWN YARD AND HE IS ENTITLED TO STOP.** His name is on nothing and was not said again in this batch. **THE ONE REFERENCE TO THE SLIP IN CHAPTERS 331 TO 340 IS AT `340`, IN A PERSON OF THAT PLACE'S MOUTH, AT A CROSS, STANDING NEXT TO HIS OWN BAG, AND HE GIVES NEITHER FIGURE AND SAYS THAT NOBODY IN THAT COUNTY HAS EVER WRITTEN DOWN THE NUMBER OF LOADS IN A YEAR AND THAT IT IS ABOUT A WEEK OF A CLERK'S WORK AND THERE IS NO CLERK IN THAT COUNTY. THAT REFERENCE IS SPENT.**
3. **THE LINE.** Nine diggers on a rise behind the last of eleven houses, and a rule written nowhere, and nobody who can say who dug the first hole. **Open, and on the eighty-sixth morning a stranger stood at the near gate for nine minutes on a dry morning and the field did not know.** The man of about seventy-four in that hamlet is in his own house and has not been out of it since the last day of the sixty-eighth week, which is Chapter 327 and his seventy-seventh morning, nine days before he stood in the field. **THE DELIVERED TEXT OF CHAPTER 336 SAID *the eleventh morning of the sixty-seventh week*, WHICH IS NOT A DAY THAT CANNOT EXIST, AND IT IS FIXED.** The woman of thirty is the ninth and has now said what she wants three times and the ninth word of the third time is **mine**.
4. **THE FUND.** A levy on loads at a bar in the tenth village, a column on a shelf in a room over a smithy, and nobody chosen to hold it. **Open, and it is the arrangement this band put a person into, and no figure is named anywhere in this batch, and the man of about thirty-eight is four days up a road with a bag and a bad back and has not decided about a book and nobody has asked him.** The book came apart in a stranger's hands in nine seconds and was put back at the height a dead woman's grandmother had it at.
5. **THE MILE.** Nine householders, ninety years, each a length, unpaid, and a man of about thirty with a cart and a beast who is the third of the nine. **Open, and a tenth person is on that road who was never asked, and a barrow at the fourth house has been going since the fifth hour because a widow of about sixty-eight put it down in the spring and told nobody, and nobody in that lane knows.**
6. **THE PERSON.** A board in a porch with eleven names on it, undated, no column, no heading, no mark against any of the eleven, in a valley of about sixty people two miles off a track off the lane, behind a bridge with one rail on it. **Open, and NOT ADJUDICATED, and the woman of about seventy-four said it again in a different shape on the eighty-ninth morning and did not concede it, and said out loud that she is not going to concede it and that he is not going to be able to settle it, and he did not settle it and did not go to the porch and did not look at the board.** Eleven names were not touched.

### THE DOCUMENTS THAT EXIST AFTER CHAPTER 340, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** Above. 64,083 at the last morning, two hands and a third in chalk, and the third has not been added to in eighty-nine days and nobody has told him to wipe it off and he has not offered. **THE FIGURE HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER AND TWO CARRIERS AND NO OTHERS: THE WRITER IS OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, AT THE SECOND LIGHT, AND HE GETS IT ONE OF TWO WAYS — he reads it himself off the wall at the foot of the ladder (332, 334, 340) or the man of about thirty-eight brings it about the second hour, and that man is four days up a road in this band and does not appear in any of the ten chapters. A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND AND MUST NOT ADD A THIRD CARRIER. **HE WAS AT COLDHARROW AT THE SECOND LIGHT IN THREE OF THE TEN MORNINGS AND IN THE OTHER SEVEN HE WAS NOT AND EACH OF THOSE SEVEN SAYS WHY IN ITS OWN FIRST OR SECOND PARAGRAPH.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it. **Not mentioned in any of the forty chapters of this volume and it is still standing.**
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines, copied and sealed at Sallowby at threepence, under the seat of a timber cart. **Not mentioned.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in a woman's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow, with sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten in pencil at the foot. **Not mentioned in any of the forty chapters of this volume.**
5. **FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **Not opened, not refused, not mentioned.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, run since he was twelve, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year, not copied and not shown. A question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered, and a third place he can see and has never been able to make out. **HE READ AN ORDER OUT LOUD IN A KITCHIN IN CHAPTER 335, UNASKED, AND THE ORDER IS A ROUND OF TURNS AND NOT A LIST OF PLACES, AND THE ROUND HE READ HAS SEVEN TURNS IN THIS ORDER: THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE WOMAN OF THIRTY, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY WITH THE CART, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY-FOUR, AND A PERSON NOBODY IN ELEVEN VILLAGES HAS COUNTED — SO THE WOMAN OF THIRTY IS THE SECOND TURN AND THE UNCOUNTED PERSON IS THE SEVENTH AND THE LAST, AND HE STOPPED FOR FOUR SECONDS AT THE SEVENTH AND WOULD NOT READ A NAME OUT OF IT BECAUSE THERE IS NOT ONE. **THE DELIVERED VERSION OF THIS ITEM SAID THE NINTH THING AND THE TENTH, WHICH IS FALSE AND WHICH CAME OUT OF THE BATCH 0004 CARD, WHICH WAS ITSELF IMPOSSIBLE, THERE BEING SIX ARRANGEMENTS.** HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THERE IS A QUESTION IN THE BOOK WITH A MARK ON THE END OF IT AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT IT IS, AND THAT THERE IS A THIRD PLACE HE CANNOT MAKE OUT, AND HE PUT TWO FINGERS ON THE COVER WHEN A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE OPENED HIS MOUTH. NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT EITHER. NO COUNT OF THE QUESTION IS PRINTED IN ANY FILE.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures. **Not mentioned.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned and not summed and it may not be summed.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned and it gained no place and lost no place.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Not mentioned.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed, and the two-syllable word at the head of a clerk's column. **Not mentioned.**
12. **THE MARKET'S FOOLSCAP BOOK AND THE SLIP IN IT.** One book, one slip, one number, on a shelf in a back room at the end of the main street of a market town four days up the lane. **In a house that has been there for a hundred and forty years, and it has not left that house in that time except about once a year. It came out of the shelf on the eighty-third morning, in a stranger's hands, and came apart at the stitching, and was put back at the height a dead woman's grandmother had it at.**
13. **AND THE THINGS THIS BAND PUT IN THE WORLD THAT ARE NOT DOCUMENTS: a sentence carried in a woman's mouth from a bar to a market cross; a record with a person in it and no form; a widow's hip and a barrow in a lane; a question a man of sixty-three asked in his own kitchen that nobody could answer; a round of turns that stops at a person nobody in eleven villages has counted; and a widow of about sixty-eight who is not even on stage and is a hip in a house.**

### THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THIS BAND DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-ninth week is 203 to 209 and the seventieth is 210 to 216. Batch 0004 ran Shelf days 206 to 215, the eighty-first morning to the ninetieth. NOTHING TURNS THE GRAIN YEAR IN THIS BAND, Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a new grain year and it is a hundred and twenty-five days after the last morning of this band, and no chapter mentions the turn and no chapter mentions the year.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed. AND A WEIGHT IS A WEIGHT IN THE COUNTY THE PLANK IS IN, AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THE TENTH VILLAGE AND THE HAMLET ARE IN THIS COUNTY AND THE MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE IS NOT, AND CHAPTER 338 SAYS SO IN ITS OWN FIRST PARAGRAPH. FOUR BELLS IS THE ONE WEIGHT THAT IS NOT PLACE-BOUND, AND CHAPTER 336 SAYS THAT IN ITS OWN WORDS. A DAY CARRIES EACH KIND OF WEIGHT ONCE, SO A SECOND MARKET IN A SECOND COUNTY IS NOT A SECOND MARKET.**

**AND THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS, UNCHANGED: four bells a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last; a stranger is three; a market is four; a boundary is seven; a debt called in is two; a fire is two; a burial is one. A LATER WRITER SHOULD TREAT THE TABLE AS SETTLED.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is eighty-nine days old and has not been added to. The market's own book is a second count in a second county and it is sixty years old and the slip in it is one market season and nobody in that town knows what the year is.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this band.**

|---|---|

| **A carter's year** | NOT NAMED. No carter's day and no carter's year appear in Chapters 331 to 340. |
| **A bell, hung, with rope, and the difference of one pound ten** | NOT NAMED. **A BELL IN THE COLDHARROW TOWER WAS NOT RUNG ON THE NINETIETH MORNING OF A MARKET DAY AND NO FIGURE IS ATTACHED TO IT, WHICH IS THE ONE TIME IN FOUR BANDS THE TEMPTATION WAS IN THE ROOM.** |
| **The levy at a bar** | NOT NAMED A THIRD TIME. A levy on loads is referred to in Chapters 337 and 338 with no unit and no count and no product, and the figure is spent. |
| **A copy** | NOT NAMED. Chapter 333 says there is no sheet in the county and no scrivener within nine miles of that smithy, and prices nothing. |
| **The market's figure** | NOT NAMED. Neither slip figure appears in Chapters 331 to 340. The one reference at `340` gives neither and says the year's count has never been written down. |
| **The difference, if it were made up out of the poor list** | NOT PRICED. Ninety people in nine towns and no product anywhere. |
| **The four hundred and eighty-five pounds** | NOT NAMED, and it is not going to be named. |

### EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **A corridor is standing in a valley of about a hundred and forty people four hundred miles off and is not going to be torn up. A man of forty called Corvin Hale signed it out loud with the working printed, is alive in his own field, and is owed a bell nobody bought.** Not mentioned. Not undone.
2. **A status is on the inside of a door in a village of about ninety people and it has no gate, no toll, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, and about four people in a room understood that and about nine did not.** Not mentioned.
3. **Four people in two counties can be asked about something, twice as many as there were fifty days ago, and one of them is about sixty and there is nobody coming, and one of them cannot write and shut her gate bar for four seconds because a hen was out.** They were not counted in this band and are not to be counted in any file.
4. **A word is not a person, and the instrument for turning a word into a person is a name at the foot of a thing.** **AND THE BAND ADDED THE OTHER HALF: a name said out loud in a street is not a name in a place, and a name written on the back of a bill by a man who did not know he was writing it is not a name in a place either, and a person of a place carrying a sentence for another place IS a person in a record, and that is the first instrument this book has found that works and not one anybody can put down.**
5. **A column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and may not be summed, and what the figures count is not known to anybody.** Not mentioned in this volume.
6. **A right of refusal that is a permission with no second half is unrestored and nobody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the forty chapters of this volume.**
7. **A bell in the March is unhung and a bell in Draymoor is owed and is not rung, and the difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten, and the difference was said out loud in a kitchen in Ashgill in Chapter 303 and has not been said since and was not said in this band.**
8. **Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead and is not named again. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended.**
9. **The carrying is off nothing. The leg is the leg. No chapter in this band prints a count of days since the leg cleared, and the leg is used as a consequence in nine of the ten chapters.**
10. **The Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half in this band and which is not named in any of the ten chapters.**
11. **The question what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network is asked in its stated form NOWHERE, and all three shapes it took in Volume 06 are spent, and `outline/volume-07.md:104` forbids a fourth.**
12. **Why the missing positions answer in a voice that sounds like the System is asked once, near the end of the volume, and is not answered. IT IS NOT ASKED IN THIS BAND AND NOBODY SPECULATES IT.**
13. **THE MIDPOINT IS SPENT AND NOTHING ABOUT IT MAY BE SPENT AGAIN. The arrangement did not stop, the untruth is the paper, the honest version is four sentences, about ninety people cannot pay it, the man of about seventy is entitled to stop, and the name of the slip's season is not to be taken out of the slip.**
14. **THE HEARING IS STILL SIX PEOPLE CARRYING IT AND IT HAS NOT HAPPENED. About six people have agreed to be somewhere on a day, the time has been changed twice, the only proposal is still a man of thirty-four's that the six be put in an order — AND A BOY OF SEVENTEEN'S BOOK HAS NOW READ THAT ORDER ALOUD AND SAID THAT IT IS THE ONLY THING THAT WORKS AND THAT A BOOK CAN ORDER THINGS AND CANNOT DATE THEM. He is not going to be in it, and the reason is no longer a leg: a week ago the reason was a leg and the reason now is a woman of about thirty-four at a bar who cannot say no and is not going to be thanked, and if he is in that room then it is a man who put her in it in order to be somewhere himself, and there is no form in four hundred miles that stops a man from doing that. **THE DELIVERED CHAPTER 340 CLOSED BY RESTATING CHAPTER 330'S CLAUSE ABOUT SIX PEOPLE AND A DAY CHANGED TWICE, ALMOST VERBATIM AND A FULL BATCH LATER, AND THAT PARAGRAPH IS GONE.****
15. **A BURDEN CARRIED BY TWO PEOPLE IS A BURDEN NEITHER OF THEM CAN PUT DOWN ALONE, and the person is now a woman of about thirty-four at a bar, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles, and he has been looking for one for ninety mornings, and she said it at a cross in the open at about the second hour on the sixth morning of the seventieth week, which is his ninetieth morning, and he was not there.**
16. **Draymoor is not one of the six and he did not go there in any of the four bands, and the finding that belongs to it was said in one paragraph in Chapter 306 and rests there.**
17. **AND THE SIX PLACES ARE FIXED AND UNCHANGED AND THE SELF-ARRIVED CLASS IS ZERO AND IT DID NOT WIDEN, AND NOBODY WAS SENT FOR ANYWHERE IN THIS BAND.**

### THE SIX THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **The morning figure appears in THREE of the ten chapters and not in seven, and a reader who expects ten will think seven are missing. They are not missing. They are the carrier rule working, and each of the seven says why in its own words.**
2. **The chain, and the rule that the morning in row *N* is row *N-1* plus the OWN DAY OF CHAPTER *N-1*, and that a table with the ring on the wrong row is a defect this project has been bitten six times.**
3. **The interval in Chapter 337 is NINETEEN DAYS, and it was stated five ways in the delivered band — *eighteen days*, *a fortnight*, *eleven days* twice, and *the middle of the last three weeks* twice — and this file and `state/current.md` both certified the whole of it as settled on eighteen days, because a sweep checks a phrase against a table and two phrases against each other is a different instrument, and because a table written by the same hand as the prose is not a check on the prose. The morning in question is `318:77`, Chapter 318 is his sixty-eighth morning, Chapter 337 is his eighty-seventh, and 87 − 68 = 19. Her own thirty days is Chapter 318's eleven plus the nineteen.**
4. **The numeric hedge is NINETY-TWO and not a hundred and twelve and not two hundred and twenty-one, and the method is the one declared at `state/current.md` §3.12 and the per-chapter table is 8 / 2 / 6 / 13 / 14 / 9 / 8 / 6 / 9 / 17 against 22,032 words, one in every two hundred and thirty-nine, and every one of the ninety-two is the age of a person.**
5. **The span scan at seventy is 122 distinct, it was 142 at delivery and the first writing was 410, and the review cut six closing paragraphs that each carried a second copy of the day's own weight, and the cause was the age phrase and not the motifs, and a later pass that reads the residue as a set of motifs will read it wrong.**
6. **The count of people who know he cannot read is printed nowhere, and the string *cannot read* is at zero over Chapters 311 to 340, and the number of *man of thirty-one* is twenty-two and falling for a seventh band.**

## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 321–330, *The Midpoint, And It Is A False Witness*. **THE FACT BASE FOR EVERYTHING AFTER IT. The block below it, `## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 311–320, *The Six Places*`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase. The live record with the map in both calendars, the board chain, the fifteen prohibitions with their file and line, the instruments with their patterns and the money with the working beside every figure is the top block of `state/current.md` under `## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0003 IS DELIVERED`, and it outranks this block.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the state of the Briar March, of a lane below it, of a valley off it and of a market town four days up that lane, at the end of Chapter 330, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the ten chapters. Every figure was measured against the ten chapter files after the prose was written.**

### THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE THIRD MORNING OF THE SIXTY-NINTH WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, and a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it.** The lane runs from village to village and not from north to south, through eleven villages. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and there is one kitchen in it that anybody will let a stranger stand in, and it is the kitchen at the end of the lane, and there are always nine people in it and the ninth is the smith's wife and the tenth never comes.** Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks. The tenth village is eleven miles down and off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps no plank.

**AND THE ONE PLACE THIS BAND PUT ON THE MAP THAT WAS NOT ON IT BEFORE, IN FULL, BECAUSE SEVEN OF THE TEN CHAPTERS STAND IN IT:**

**A MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE, in a county no living person in this book has stood in. A long main street with a cross at the top of it and a market cross on a step worn in the middle; a weighbridge and a set of beam scales at the end of the street on a stone plinth that has been re-cut three times in a hundred years; a standpipe at the end of Church Lane that has never once been off; a bridge; a churchyard at the far end of the town behind a long dry wall four feet high with lime in the joints; a cross at the town cross; a toll box with a slot in it; a foolscap book on a shelf in a back room at the end of the main street, in a house that has been there longer than the plinth, with a slip in it. ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE LIVE IN THIS TOWN. Four hundred loads go over that bridge in a week in the season and a season is about twelve weeks, **and a year is that season and the other forty weeks, and the other forty weeks carry as much again, and the market does NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED LOADS IN A YEAR.** There is no plank at the end of that street and there has never been one and there is no figure on any wall in that town and there never has been. There is no bell in that town that anybody rings for anything. Nine towns share a poor list that is kept at the cross, and the list carries about ninety names, and four of the ninety are in this town.**

### THE ARRANGEMENT AS IT STANDS AFTER CHAPTER 330, WHICH IS THE THING A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW

**The scales and the toll. A share of every load that crosses the bridge pays for the standard of the load kept on the plinth and for the water and the bridge and the wall and the cross. The share is worked out every year in a back room at the end of the main street by dividing the cost of those things by the number of loads in a year, and THE NUMBER OF LOADS IN A YEAR IS NOT WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE IN THAT TOWN, and what is written down is one market season's count, on a slip in a foolscap book that has been in that house for a hundred and forty years, and that season's count has been divided into the year's cost every year for sixty years.**

**THE SLIP SAYS FOUR THOUSAND EIGHT HUNDRED AND IT IS ONE SEASON. THE MARKET DOES NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED IN A YEAR. THE SLIP IS EXACTLY HALF OF THE YEAR. THE SLIP IS SIXTY YEARS OLD. The difference has paid for the water and the bridge and the wall and the cross every year since, and about four hundred people have drunk, crossed and been buried on the strength of it, and every load has paid twice what it should.**

**AND THIS BLOCK PREVIOUSLY PRINTED *THE MARKET DOES ABOUT NINE THOUSAND*, AND THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND THAT THE REPAIR WAS OBLIGATORY RATHER THAN COSMETIC, BECAUSE THE BAND'S OWN SENTENCES GAVE FOUR HUNDRED LOADS A WEEK IN A TWELVE-WEEK SEASON, WHICH IS THE SLIP'S FIGURE EXACTLY, AND 4,800 AGAINST 9,000 IS FIFTY-THREE PER CENT AND NOT *LESS THAN HALF*, SO ON THE PRINTED FIGURES THE SLIP WAS A TRUE NUMBER FOR A YEAR IT WAS NEVER MEANT TO MEASURE AND THE REVERSAL HAD NO FALSE WITNESS UNDER IT AT ALL. A SEASON IN THE SLIP IS THE BETTER FALSE WITNESS AND IT IS THE SAME STORY.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY IS CALLED WENLOCK. He is seventy and he has been at that plinth about forty-four years, has his hands behind his back, does not do the sums and has not done them for about nine years, and his daughter of about fifty-eight has done them since she was forty-nine and is six months gone with a child and will be stood up at the spring. He was eight years old when his father told him in that yard that the number on the slip was right, and he was not in the room, and the man who wrote the slip is dead, and he has been the only one who could check it since he was about nine, and he checks it by seeing whether the things on the slip are paid for — the water, the bridge, the wall and the cross — and they always have been, and **that is a check on a consequence and not on a count, which is why it told him the slip was wrong and never told him by how much, and he said the year's figure out loud for the first time in Chapter 324 out of forty-four years of standing on that stone and said so: *that is out of forty-four years of standing on that stone and not out of a book, and I asked a stranger for it yesterday morning at about the ninth hour and he did not have it either.* THE FIGURE HE GAVE WAS NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED, AND NOBODY IN THAT TOWN CAN CHECK IT, INCLUDING HIM.** He said it out loud once, in his own yard, in the rain, on the fourth morning of the sixty-eighth week, to a woman of about sixty-one who asked him in her own doorway. Nobody has asked him to say it again and nobody is going to. HIS NAME IS ON NOTHING AND IT IS NOW SPOKEN IN ONE STREET BY ONE PERSON.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-ONE OWNS THE GROUND THE PLINTH STANDS ON AND THE HOUSE THE BOOK IS IN. She has been there fifty years, she has never been asked what the slip is for, two of the four names she can put a finger on are her own, and she has said out loud that she is not going to be the person who says the water stops and that she is not going to be asked to choose.**

**AND NOTHING HAS BEEN DONE ABOUT ANY OF IT. The toll is still being taken. The beam is still on the plinth. About nine loads go over in the first hour. The standpipe is running. The wall is standing. The number is on the slip. About nine people know it and nine thousand six hundred loads a year go over a beam that is calibrated by one season's count that has been called a year's for sixty years, and two men in a tavern at the end of the main street have been saying all week that the water ought to be paid for by the people who use it, and neither of them was in the yard.**

### THE DOCUMENTS THAT EXIST AFTER CHAPTER 330, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** Above. **63,955 at the last printed figure and 64,018 at the last morning, two hands and a third in chalk, and the third has not been added to in seventy-nine days and nobody has told him to wipe it off and he has not offered.** **THE FIGURE HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER AND TWO CARRIERS AND NO OTHERS: THE WRITER IS OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, AT THE SECOND LIGHT, AND HE GETS IT ONE OF TWO WAYS — he reads it himself at the foot of the ladder (321, 322) or the man of about thirty-eight brings it about the second hour, having been at the foot of the ladder at the second light four days down the lane because he does it four times a week and was passing through on his way to four valleys away (324, 325, 326, 327, 328, 329, 330). A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND TO IT AND MUST NOT ADD A THIRD CARRIER, AND MUST NOT HAVE ANYBODY ELSE CARRY IT INTO THAT MARKET TOWN.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it. **Not mentioned in any of the thirty chapters of this volume and it is still standing.**
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines, copied and sealed at Sallowby at threepence, under the seat of a timber cart. **Not mentioned.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in Sable Venn's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow, with sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten in pencil at the foot. **Not mentioned in any of the thirty chapters of this volume.**
5. **FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **Not opened, not refused, not mentioned.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, run since he was twelve, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year, not copied and not shown. A question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered, and a third place he can see and has never been able to make out. **HE DID NOT APPEAR IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BAND AND HE WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT HIS THIRD PLACE AND HE DID NOT WANT TO BE, AND THE QUESTION IN HIS BOOK IS SEVENTY-NINE DAYS OLD AND NO COUNT OF IT IS PRINTED IN ANY FILE. `outline/volume-07.md:76` PUTS HIS BOOK READING AN ORDER THAT A DOCUMENT CANNOT IN BATCH 0004, AND IT HAS NOT AND MAY NOT BE MADE TO.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures. **Not mentioned.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned and not summed and it may not be summed.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned and it gained no place and lost no place.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Not mentioned.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed, and the two-syllable word at the head of a clerk's column. **Not mentioned.**
12. **THE MARKET'S FOOLSCAP BOOK AND THE SLIP IN IT.** One book, one slip, one number. **In a back room at the end of the main street of a market town four days up the lane, in a house that has been there for a hundred and forty years, and it has not left that house in that time except about once a year, and it came out twice in forty-four years and the man of about seventy has read it twice.** It is a piece of paper and a person can be asked about it, and the person is a woman of about sixty-one.
13. **AND THE THINGS THIS BAND PUT IN THE WORLD THAT ARE NOT DOCUMENTS:** nine people of a market town and one name, said out loud at a plinth; a count of ninety names on a list at a cross in nine towns; a wall of four feet that a man of about forty-one has been mending out of his own lime for eleven years; a woman of about thirty-two counting on a standpipe for her second child; a man of about sixty-three counting on a standpipe for four acres; and the finding that a thing which has had to be said out loud in a yard is a thing that can be said again, and two men in a tavern said it that way first.

### THE PEOPLE OF THIS BAND, AND NONE OF THEM IS AN ANTAGONIST AND NONE OF THEM IS RELIEVED AND NONE OF THEM IS REDEEMED AND NONE OF THEM WAS THANKED

**ILYAN VESTER, THIRTY-ONE. THE ARC OF THE BAND IN ONE SENTENCE: he stood at a bridge with one rail on it and was not rescued and was not thanked and did not ask a woman of seventy-four for anything and did not adjudicate what she had not conceded, heard a man of about thirty-eight say that a fund is a levy and that the honest version is worse than the lie, stood at a bridge in a market street for four hours in the rain and said nothing, did not use the only instrument he owns and said why, was not in a yard when the figure came out and did not say one word for the hour, let a woman of about sixty-one ask a question he had come to ask, watched a sum get done that he is not allowed to be given **and watched a man of about seventy work out in a yard, out loud, in front of four people, the one number in the band that nobody can check, out of forty-four years of standing on a stone,**n, watched a second sum not close, heard a man of about thirty-eight say that he is not a villain and is not being brave, spent a market day being asked nothing, found out that nothing stopped, was given a name that is not on anything by a man of seventy in a street, went and got two people out of ninety and did not say the figure to either of them, decided not to put ninety names on a paper, found out that the price of the method is that a thing said in a yard can be said again, was told by a woman of fifty-eight one sentence that was not about him, and is going back four days down a lane to a porch in about nine days with nothing to show for any of it except a sentence he said to a wall in a lane.** Stage 4, held and not re-arrived, not stat'd. `Remedy Drafter` returns zero and the string `stage` returns zero.

**OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, the woman of forty-one at the board at Coldharrow. SHE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS BAND AT ALL AND WAS NOT ASKED TO BE IN ONE, and the plank was written on nine of the ten mornings by the two carriers at §Document 1 and she was in none of the ten chapters, and that is a finding and not an oversight.** Keeps the bell and the board, twenty-five years, ninepence a day for a hundred and forty days, five pounds five shillings a year. The third hand in the chalk is seventy-nine days old and nothing was added to it in this band and nobody has told him to wipe it off. **AND HE STILL DOES NOT KNOW THAT SHE SAID THE SUM OUT LOUD ON A LADDER ON THE EVENING BEFORE CHAPTER 301 FOR THE FIRST TIME IN TWENTY-FIVE YEARS, AND NOTHING IN CHAPTERS 321 TO 330 TELLS HIM, AND A LATER WRITER MAY NOT TELL HIM, AND IF IT IS EVER TELLABLE IT IS TELLABLE BY HER AND NOT BY A NARRATOR.** She used the phrase *would like it noticed* zero times in this band and the string *would like* is at zero over the ten chapters.

**A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WITH A CART, the counter of other people's money. He is in seven of the ten chapters, on his own account, passing through on his way to four valleys away, and nobody sent for him and nobody thanked him and he is not a villain and he is not a martyr and he says in a lane that he is not being brave.** In this band he said that the fund is not a fund and never was one and is a levy, **and he did the second sum over nine thousand six hundred loads against the slip's four thousand eight hundred and it does not close, and he says in the sentence that three pence a load is the wrong instrument in this county and that there is no other instrument in it, and he has not got the number of loads in that market and has not got it in nine of the eleven villages the fund runs on,** and that he has counted a column for eleven years and never once asked what the column is a column of, and the second half that frightens him, which is that he has checked the other ten villages four times in eleven years and has never once found a hole in one of them. He carried the morning figure up four days of lane seven times. He did two sums out loud and printed neither. He said the batch's finding about the ninety in his own mouth on the last morning and then went out of that market on a cart at a quarter past the seventh hour and has not decided about the book and was not going to decide in a lane at a cross. **He is owed nothing and nobody has chosen him to keep anything and he walked out with a bag and a bad back and asked a man of thirty-one to walk him to the cross, and the man of thirty-one said he would and nobody thanked either of them.**

**A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AT A BAR, TWENTY-TWO YEARS, eleven miles down and off the lane in the tenth village. SHE IS IN ONE CHAPTER OF THIS BAND, in Chapter 322, and she said the thing nobody had been chosen before he asked, and he did not ask her a third thing.** She is not one of the six and there is no arrangement in this county that keeps her. She was not thanked and said in Chapter 312 that she would not be.

**A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT A BAR, twenty-two years, at the bottom end of the lane. SHE IS IN ONE CHAPTER OF THIS BAND, in Chapter 322, reading him off her own board in four seconds and not asking his business.** The thread that nobody has ever asked her a second question is still open and was not spent.

**BRAM ORREN, SEVENTEEN.** Did not appear in this band. Asked nothing about his third place and did not want two things asked at once. Not spent.

**TARIN KEEL, FIFTY-TWO.** **DID NOT APPEAR IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BAND AND WAS NOT USED AND WAS NOT RESOLVED, and his answer to *what were you the only person in* is not given and is a Batch 0004 thing, and the paragraph that plants the volume's hearing is his and it is one paragraph and it rests where it is and was not returned to.** He has still not corrected the hundred and forty days and a later pass may not quietly correct it and may not quietly use 110.

**SERA QUILL, TWENTY-EIGHT, the recordwright.** **NOT MENTIONED IN ANY OF THE THIRTY CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 07 AND NOT ON STAGE IN ANY OF THEM, and that is now the third band running and it is structural.** Her family claim of six years is unfiled and was not mentioned. **A Batch 0004 card that wants her has to earn her, and the temptation to put the volume's archivist in the room where a hearing is held is exactly the temptation Volume 06's instrument is made of, and this band went seven days in a market street without her and the street was full of people who said things in their own mouths without her.**

**SABLE VENN. A WOMAN. DOES NOT APPEAR IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS NOT NAMED AND IS NOT MENTIONED, and that is the fifth consecutive band and it is correct and it is not a defeat.** She is a woman with a bag and about thirty people of her own who carries a fund's book to a smithy nine times a year and has done for six years, and she is named in one line of Chapter 330 in another man's mouth as a person who is not going to be asked to keep it, and **she is not rescued, not thanked, not relieved, not redeemed, not defeated, not sent for, and not a mechanism, and the line she is in is not about her and is about a book.**

### THE NINE NEW PEOPLE OF THIS BAND, AND NONE OF THEM IS A PRODUCTION

**1. A WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-ONE, whose yard the plinth stands in and whose back room the market's book is in.** A woman. Fifty years in that house. She asked the question the midpoint turned on, in her own yard, and she has two of the ninety on a list at a cross, and she said out loud that she is not going to be the person who says the water stops and that she is not going to be asked to choose. Not relieved. Not thanked.
**2. A WOMAN OF TWENTY-NINE who looks after the cross and keeps the list.** A woman. Nine years. She is the only person in this band who can be checked on a number, and the number is about ninety, and she said the batch's finding in a lane unasked: *they are not going to be charged more, they are going to be told that the thing they have been counting on was never theirs, and there is no form in this country that says a person may say that a thing was never theirs.* Not thanked, and she said so first.
**3. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO with a pitch at the cross, expecting her second in the spring.** A woman. Nine years at that pitch. One of the four the woman of twenty-nine can name. He stood at her pitch for nine minutes and bought nothing and said nothing at all about the figure, and that is the batch's finding the second time. Not relieved.
**4. A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FIVE with a pitch at the cross, with a debt called in on her on the fourth morning of the week.** A woman. Settled it by both of them being tired. Not a villain and not relieved and nothing happened to her.
**5. A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE who mends four feet of wall in a lane out of his own lime.** A man. Eleven years. It has been down four times and it is always the same four feet. He gave the best answer anybody gave in the band and it was about his mother. **He is not the sixth place and he is not in a line of nine and he has not been asked for anything and nobody thanked him, and a chapter in this band is about him and a wall and nothing else, and that is the reason that chapter exists.**
**6. A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE with four acres and eggs, one of the four the woman of twenty-nine can name.** A man. One of the ninety. He has been on a list at a cross for forty years and has never been asked what the list is for and said it is for the field. He told a stranger off and was not thanked and did not want to be.
**7. A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-TWO from a town nine miles off, who called in a debt.** A man. In one morning and out of the street.
**8. A CARTER from a town nine miles off who stood at the cross for nine minutes and bought nothing.** A man. **HE IS NOT THE STRANGER IN CHAPTER 329'S DAY AND THE DELIVERY'S OWN STATE FILES SAID HE WAS, WHICH THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 CORRECTED: a stranger arriving in this market town four days up the lane is not a March weight, and the stranger in that day's weight is in a village four days the other way and has never been seen. The carter is a stranger in this street and that is a different fact and it is the reason he stands at the cross.**
**9. WENLOCK, the man of about seventy.** A man. Seventy. Forty-four years at that plinth. His name is on nothing and it is now said out loud once, in a street, in nine words, at a plinth, in the rain, and one person in that town has used it since. **He is not a villain, he is not a criminal, he is not going to be asked to say it again, and he is entitled to stop.**

### THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THIS BAND DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-eighth week is 196 to 202 and the sixty-ninth is 203 to 209. Batch 0003 ran Shelf days 196 to 205, the seventy-first morning to the eightieth. NOTHING TURNS THE GRAIN YEAR IN THIS BAND, Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a new grain year and it is a hundred and fourteen days after the last morning of this batch, and no chapter mentions the turn and no chapter mentions the year.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed. AND A WEIGHT IS A WEIGHT IN THE COUNTY THE PLANK IS IN, AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THIS BLOCK PREVIOUSLY SAID THE OPPOSITE AND WAS WRONG. `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0316.md:7` is the authority and says it in the fiction — *a fire in a market four days up a lane is not a March weight and is not on that plank at all* — and `316:3` and `323:3` say the same of the FIGURE, which does not travel either. A MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE IS IN A COUNTY NO LIVING PERSON IN THIS BOOK HAS STOOD IN, AND ITS OWN MARKET, ITS OWN DEBT CALLED IN, ITS OWN BURIAL, ITS OWN FIRE AND ITS OWN STRANGER ARE NOT ON THE PLANK, AND CHAPTERS 323, 324, 325, 326, 327 AND 329 ALL SAID OTHERWISE AT DELIVERY. ALL SIX ARE NOW RIGHT AND EACH SAYS WHICH MARKET THE WEIGHT BELONGS TO AND WHICH ONE IS RUNNING IN THE STREET. FOUR BELLS IS THE ONE WEIGHT THAT IS NOT PLACE-BOUND. A DAY CARRIES EACH KIND OF WEIGHT ONCE, SO A SECOND DEBT CALLED IN IN A SECOND COUNTY IS NOT A SECOND WEIGHT.**

**AND THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS, WHICH IS UNCHANGED BY THIS BAND AND WHICH CHAPTER 314 OF BATCH 0002 PRINTED IN ONE SENTENCE, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN THE HISTORY OF THIS PROJECT THAT THE WHOLE TABLE HAS BEEN IN THE FICTION: four bells a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last; a stranger is three; a market is four; a boundary is seven; a debt called in is two; a fire is two; a burial is one. A LATER WRITER SHOULD TREAT THE TABLE AS SETTLED AND SHOULD NOT ADD AN EIGHTH WEIGHT WITHOUT A FIGURE THAT NEEDS IT.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, and a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is seventy-nine days old and has not been added to. The market's own book is a second count in a second county and it is sixty years old and it has a slip in it with a number on the slip and nobody in that town knows what the number is a count of, **and after the review of 27 September 2026 everybody who knows knows exactly what it is a count of, which is one market season, and the finding is that a true number kept for the wrong year is the hardest kind of false witness to find, because checking it means checking the year, and there is no book with the year in it.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this band.**

### EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **A corridor is standing in a valley of about a hundred and forty people four hundred miles off and is not going to be torn up. A man of forty called Corvin Hale signed it out loud with the working printed, is alive in his own field, and is owed a bell nobody bought.** Not mentioned in this volume. Not undone.
2. **A status is on the inside of a door in a village of about ninety people and it has no gate, no toll, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, and about four people in a room understood that and about nine did not.** Not mentioned.
3. **Four people in two counties can be asked about something, twice as many as there were fifty days ago, and one of them is about sixty and there is nobody coming, and one of them cannot write and shut her gate bar for four seconds because a hen was out.** They were not counted in this band and are not to be counted in any file.
4. **A word is not a person, and the instrument for turning a word into a person is a name at the foot of a thing, and he offered it once in a room in Volume 06 and was refused, and in Chapter 303 a man of sixty-three gave him one without asking, and in Chapter 328 a man of about seventy gave him one that is on nothing at all, and the finding of that chapter is that a name said out loud at a plinth in a street is not a name in a place and the two of them together do not make a person.** He is on no paper in this band.
5. **A column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and may not be summed, and what the figures count is not known to anybody.** Not mentioned in this volume.
6. **A right of refusal that is a permission with no second half is unrestored and nobody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the thirty chapters of this volume. And the false witness of Chapter 324 is not that and has nothing to do with it.**
7. **A bell in the March is unhung and a bell in Draymoor is owed and is still not rung, and the difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten, and the difference was said out loud in a kitchen in Ashgill in Chapter 303 and has not been said since.**
8. **Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended.**
9. **The carrying is off nothing. The leg is the leg. No chapter in this band prints a count of days since the leg cleared, and the leg is used as a consequence in nine of the ten chapters.**
10. **The Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half in this band and which is not named in any of the ten chapters.**
11. **The question what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network is asked in its stated form NOWHERE in this band, and all three shapes it took in Volume 06 are spent, and `outline/volume-07.md:104` forbids a fourth.**
12. **Why the missing positions answer in a voice that sounds like the System is asked once, near the end of the volume, and is not answered. IT IS NOT ASKED IN THIS BAND AND NOBODY SPECULATES IT.**
13. **THE MIDPOINT HAS LANDED AND IT IS THE SCALES, and the remaining thing about it is that the arrangement did not stop and the honest version is on the table in a yard and about nine people know it and about ninety people cannot pay it, and the slip is still in the book and the toll is still being taken, and the man of about seventy is entitled to stop and nobody is going to ask him again. NONE OF THAT MAY BE SPENT AGAIN IN BATCH 0004.**
14. **THE HEARING IS STILL SIX PEOPLE CARRYING IT AND IT HAS NOT HAPPENED AND IT WILL NOT HAVE A DAY. About six people have agreed to be somewhere on a day, the time has been changed twice and will change again, the only proposal on the table is a man of thirty-four's that the six be put in an order, he is not going to be in it, and the band added one thing to the machinery and it is that a rota is a rota and there is one on the back of a bill in a kitchen three miles up the lane and nobody has ever read it out.**
15. **Draymoor is not one of the six and he did not go there in any of the three bands, and the finding that belongs to it was said in one paragraph in Chapter 306 and rests there.**
16. **AND THE SIX PLACES ARE FIXED AND UNCHANGED AND THE SELF-ARRIVED CLASS IS ZERO AND IT DID NOT WIDEN, and none of the nine new people of this band is to be added to any list of anything.**

THIS IS THE THIRD TIME THIS PROJECT HAS BEEN SHIPPED A PROMPT WHOSE OWN FIGURE COLUMN DISAGREES WITH ITS OWN CHAIN, and a later pass should sum both columns rather than believe either.**

The three-alternative day pattern sees seventeen references in this band and the fourth form saw three that named no week at all and are now out, and seven intervals and two days found by reading and not by any sweep were all wrong and all are corrected.**
4. **A forty-character carry-over is connective tissue and a seventy-character one is a defect in most cases, and after the review of 27 September 2026 this band has ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-THREE cross-chapter windows at seventy, and they are not to be reworded, and the figure came down from four hundred and seventy by hand in two passes and then from two hundred and twenty in the repair, and a later band should expect the same. **THE REVIEW ALSO FOUND THAT THE RESIDUE WAS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AND THE DELIVERY CALLED BOTH OF THEM MOTIFS: four mechanical tics and five declared devices, and only the tics are defects, and *nine feet off* alone was in nine of the ten chapters.**
