This band has one named table figure and two unit prices and no product, and the reversal's small false figure is a count and not a price, and a reader who wants the difference between four thousand eight hundred and nine thousand six hundred has to do it, which is the arrangement. **AND THE DIFFERENCE IS EXACTLY THE SLIP, SO A READER WHO DOES IT GETS THE WHOLE OF THE MIDPOINT IN TWO LINES, WHICH IS THE BEST THING THE REPAIR DID AND IT IS NOT A NEW FACT, IT IS THE SAME TWO NUMBERS.** And AND DO NOT LET AN INSTRUMENT OPEN A CHAPTER FILE FOR WRITING: the hedge reduction of this band was run by a script over the whole file including the heading line and it removed the word *About* from two chapter titles, which is a new instance of the same class.**

### THE REVIEW OF THIS BAND, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT, IN THIS FILE SPECIFICALLY

**A REVIEW RAN ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026. ITS FULL RECEIPT, ITEM BY ITEM, IS §10 OF THE TOP BLOCK OF `state/current.md`, WHICH IS THE AUTHORITY. ITS LOG IS `logs/batch-0003.review.log`. IT FOUND SEVEN BLOCKING AND ELEVEN IMPORTANT FINDINGS AND ALL SEVEN AND NINE OF THE ELEVEN ARE FIXED. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED AND NO PLOT WAS CHANGED.**

**THE FOUR THINGS THIS FILE HAD TO GIVE UP, AND ALL FOUR WERE ABOUT THE SAME NUMBER.**

1. **THE SLIP IS NOT A COUNT OF LOADS IN A YEAR. IT IS ONE MARKET SEASON'S COUNT, AND A YEAR IS NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED.** This file printed *the market does about nine thousand*, and six chapters gave four hundred loads a week in a twelve-week season, and the product of the band's own two numbers was the slip, so the slip was right and the midpoint had no false witness under it. Everything in this file about the slip, the share, the ratio, the toll and the beam has been rewritten, and the mechanism is now stated once, in §The arrangement as it stands, and the reader can do it.
2. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY CHECKS THE SLIP AND NOT A LIST, AND HE CHECKS A CONSEQUENCE AND NOT A COUNT.** This file said *he checks it by seeing whether the things on the list are paid for*, and no chapter says that. **THE LIST IS THE NINETY-NAME POOR LIST AND IT IS A DIFFERENT THING AND IT APPEARS ONLY IN THE HONEST VERSION OF THE ARRANGEMENT.** A state file that summarises a check nobody made will produce a false witness of its own, and that is what had happened here.
3. **A WEIGHT IS A WEIGHT IN THE COUNTY THE PLANK IS IN, AND THIS FILE SAID THE OPPOSITE.** It said a fire, a market and a debt called in in this market town were counted because the man of thirty-one was standing in the middle of them, in the same sentence as the sentence that says a stroke does not travel. Six chapters are now corrected and the rule is written down, and **four bells is the one weight that is not place-bound.**
4. **THE MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT'S SECOND SUM IS OVER NINE THOUSAND SIX HUNDRED AND HE SAYS IN THE SENTENCE THAT THREE PENCE A LOAD IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT IN THIS COUNTY.**

**AND THE TWO THINGS THIS FILE ADDS THAT ARE NOT CORRECTIONS. THE FIRST IS THE THING THE REVIEW'S B3 REPAIR CREATED AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL NEW FACT IN THE BAND: NOBODY IN THAT MARKET TOWN HAS EVER WRITTEN THE NUMBER OF LOADS IN A YEAR DOWN, the slip is a season, the year has never been counted, and the honest version of the arrangement begins with a man writing a year on a slip, which is ordinary work that nobody in that county has anybody to do. It is Batch 0004's to have or to refuse. THE SECOND IS THAT THE MAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY SAID THE YEAR'S FIGURE ON THE SECOND MORNING AFTER ASKING A STRANGER FOR IT ON THE FIRST AND BEING TOLD NO, and the figure he gave is the only one in the band that no character and no reader can check, including him, and that is now a fact of the fiction and not an oversight.**

## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 311–320, *The Six Places*. **THE ARCHIVE RECORD. The block below it, `## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 301–310, *The Seams*`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the state of the Briar March, of a lane below it, of a market four days up, of a hamlet eleven miles off it and of a valley two miles past a bridge, at the end of Chapter 320, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the ten chapters. Every figure was measured against the ten chapter files after the prose was written.**

### THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE SEVENTH AND LAST DAY OF THE SIXTY-SEVENTH WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, and a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it.** The lane runs from village to village and not from north to south, through eleven villages. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and there is one kitchen in it that anybody will let a stranger stand in, and it is the kitchen at the end of the lane, and there are always nine people in it and the ninth is the smith's wife and the tenth never comes.** Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks.

**AND THE THINGS THIS BAND ADDED TO THE MAP, all of them measured over the ten chapter files, and none of them is a document:**

- **THE TENTH VILLAGE IS NOT IN THE BRIAR MARCH AND THAT IS WHY IT HAS NO PLANK**, and this is now settled in the fiction in Chapter 316, in a sentence, in the market town: *there is no plank at the end of this street, and there is no figure on a wall at the second light, and there is no woman of forty-one here, and nobody in this market town has ever heard of a board at Coldharrow or a lane that goes from village to village.* The tenth village is eleven miles down and off the lane, it has a bar with four letters and a stroke at the top of its board, about nine loads a day at threepence, a widow's fund, a room over a smithy and a book on a shelf. **A BAR IN THE TENTH VILLAGE AND A BAR AT THE BOTTOM END OF THE LANE ARE TWO BARS IN TWO COUNTIES, and a lane of eleven villages runs between them, and the bar at the bottom of the lane counts strangers and the bar in the tenth village counts everybody.**
- **A FIRE IN A MARKET TOWN FOUR DAYS UP THE LANE IS NOT A MARCH WEIGHT AND IS NOT ON THE MARCH PLANK.** `316:7` says it in as many words. **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE CHAPTER: the day came to four bells and a fire and a debt called in, and he was standing in the middle of the one of those two things and he will hear about the other one never.** A weight on a plank is a weight in the county the plank is in.
- **THERE IS NO BELL IN THE MARKET TOWN THAT ANYBODY RINGS FOR ANYTHING**, and a man from another county notices it in four seconds, and a second ring for a fire is a thing that happens on a plank in a different county and not in that street.
- **A YARD IN COLDHARROW BEHIND THE LAST OF THE GOOD GROUND, a chapel yard, is not the ground on the rise behind the hamlet and has nothing to do with the line of nine, and a man of about forty-four digs there for the parish out of a box and is paid whatever is in the box.** He is a person of Coldharrow and is not in any of the six.
- **A GATE BAR AT THE BOTTOM END OF A LANE ELEVEN MILES DOWN AND OFF IT, held by a woman of about thirty-four for twenty-two years, is a bar and not a gate, and it is the sixth thing she is in and she is the one person in the band who is in a place where everybody turns up.**
- **THE SIX PLACES HAVE NOW BEEN VISITED BETWEEN THEM SEVEN TIMES IN TWENTY CHAPTERS AND NOBODY HAS COUNTED, and a count would be a thing a person could be entered against.**

### THE DOCUMENTS THAT EXIST AFTER CHAPTER 320, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** Above. **63,955, two hands and a third in chalk, and the third has not been added to in sixty-nine days and nobody has told him to wipe it off and he has not offered.** **THE FIGURE HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER AND TWO CARRIERS IN THE TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BAND AND NO OTHERS: THE WRITER IS OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, AT THE SECOND LIGHT, AND HE GETS IT ONE OF TWO WAYS — he reads it himself at the foot of the ladder (311, 312, 317, 318, 319) or the man of about thirty-eight brings it about the second hour, because he has been doing it about four times a week for years (313, 320). A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND TO IT AND MUST NOT ADD A THIRD CARRIER.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it. **Not mentioned in any of the twenty chapters of this volume and it is still standing.**
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines, copied and sealed at Sallowby at threepence, under the seat of a timber cart. **Not mentioned.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in Sable Venn's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow, with sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten in pencil at the foot. **Not mentioned in any of the twenty chapters of this volume, and Sable Venn appears in ONE of them, once, in Chapter 313, in a lane behind a smithy with a bag, and nothing is taken off her and nothing is said about her in her own hearing.**
5. **THE FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **Not opened, not refused, not mentioned.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, run since he was twelve, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year, not copied and not shown. A question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered. **AND HE APPEARED IN ONE OF THE TEN CHAPTERS, AT `319`, IN A YARD AT COLDHARROW, AND HE DID NOT OPEN THE BOOK, AND HE SAID ONE THING AND IT WAS THIS: *There is a third place in it I have never been able to make out, and I have not got it and I am not going to get it this year, and I want to say that out loud once in a yard with nine people in it, because nine people in this county have been told about it by somebody who was not me, and I would rather the first time it was said in a yard it was said by me and it was said all the way through.* NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT IT AND HE DID NOT WANT TO BE ASKED TWO THINGS AT ONCE, AND HIS THIRD PLACE IS NOT SPENT AND HIS BOOK HAS NOT READ AN ORDER THAT A DOCUMENT CANNOT, WHICH `outline/volume-07.md:76` RESERVES FOR BATCH 0004.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures. **Not mentioned.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned and not summed and it may not be summed.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned and it gained no place and lost no place.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Not mentioned.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed, and the two-syllable word at the head of a clerk's column. **Not mentioned.**
12. **AND THE NEW THINGS THIS BAND PUT IN THE WORLD, WHICH ARE SEVEN AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS A DOCUMENT:** a sixth arrangement said out loud in a kitchen in a row; a man of sixty-three's first question to anybody in about thirty-one years, asked eleven miles away at a bar; a second question put to a woman of about thirty-four at that bar about a mile of road, by a stranger, when the man of sixty-three had already put one; a woman of thirty with a bag carrying a book to a smithy and back; a yard in Coldharrow with a hole in the top corner of it and a boy of about eleven who dug two of the holes and was not stopped and was not counted; a woman of about forty-one at the seventh house of a lane of nine householders, doing a widow's length in the dark for eleven days; and the first description of a hearing anybody in this book has ever had — six people, no form, no room, no officer, no sheet, and a time that two of them cannot keep and a rota that a person of a place proposed instead.

### THE PEOPLE OF THIS BAND, AND NONE OF THEM IS AN ANTAGONIST AND NONE OF THEM IS RELIEVED AND NONE OF THEM IS REDEEMED AND NONE OF THEM WAS THANKED

**ILYAN VESTER, THIRTY-ONE. THE ARC OF THE BAND IN ONE SENTENCE: he put six arrangements side by side in a kitchen out loud, found out that a row is not a join, announced that he would say one thing in each of five rooms and would not be in the rooms when what he said arrived, watched the man of sixty-three walk eleven miles on a bad knee to ask a woman a question because of it, was asked a second question himself about a mile of road, gave the mile a sentence in a road and was told that is a sentence, stood in a lane behind a smithy and could see the inside of a woman's unpaid labour and did nothing about it because there is nothing to do about it, stood at one end of two things on the same morning and at neither of the important ones, did not say the one thing he had come eleven days up a lane to say, nearly counted a woman who was not on the road, and then found out that the six have been talking to each other for nine days without him — and he is going down a lane to a porch in the morning and he is not going to be thanked for that either.** Stage 4, held and not re-arrived, not stat'd. `Remedy Drafter` returns zero.

**OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, the woman of forty-one at the board at Coldharrow. She is in Chapters 311, 314, 315, 319 and 320 and she is off the stage for 312, 313, 316, 317 and 318. Nothing was taken off her and nothing was given to her and nobody thanked her.** In this band **she answered the question he did not ask her in Chapter 311's kitchen — she did not ask him anything, and Chapter 311 says so — and in Chapter 314 she told him, unasked, that a day's figure is a weighted number and cannot be turned back into what was done, and that on nine of the last hundred days she could not have told him and has never once been asked to; and in Chapter 315 she put a stroke on the plank for a burial and cannot show anybody which stroke it was and he did not ask her, and she said a dead man's name at a graveside because nobody else was going to, and nobody asked her to.** She used the phrase *would like it noticed* once, in Chapter 319, and that is inside the limit of twice in a band. **AND HE STILL DOES NOT KNOW THAT SHE SAID THE SUM OUT LOUD ON A LADDER ON THE EVENING BEFORE CHAPTER 301 FOR THE FIRST TIME IN TWENTY-FIVE YEARS, AND NOTHING IN CHAPTERS 311 TO 320 TELLS HIM, AND A LATER WRITER MAY NOT TELL HIM IN THE NEXT BATCH EITHER, AND IF IT IS EVER TELLABLE IT IS TELLABLE BY HER AND NOT BY A NARRATOR.**

**A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AT A BAR, TWENTY-TWO YEARS, four letters and a stroke, about nine loads a day at threepence, the only person in four counties that anybody goes to when they want to know a thing and does not want to be seen wanting to know it.** In this band **she was asked her first question about a place in about twenty-two years, by a man of sixty-three on a bad knee; she was asked a second question, about a mile of road, by the man of thirty-one, and she answered it herself, and she said I do not want a thing done about the mile, I want you to go and say one thing about it out loud, and she knew it would not help and she asked anyway; she went into the room over the smithy and asked the man of thirty-eight a second question in front of about nine people, unprompted; and in Chapter 320 she said the sentence the batch is built on and did not finish it and went out and opened the bar and read four loads off her own board.** She is not thanked for any of it and said in Chapter 312 that if he thanks her she will put a stroke on her board for him and he can watch her do it. She is in Chapters 312, 313, 316 (at the end of it) and 320.

**A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT A BAR, twenty-two years, at the bottom end of the lane, four letters and a stroke, and there are two such boards in this volume and they are eleven miles apart in two counties.** She counted him as a stranger on his fifty-second morning and stopped counting him a month later and has not told him and is not going to. **SHE IS IN CHAPTERS 314 AND 319 AND IS ASKED NOTHING IN EITHER, AND THE THREAD THAT NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HER A SECOND QUESTION IS STILL OPEN AND WAS NOT SPENT IN THIS BAND, AND THE BAND'S FOURTH QUESTION WENT TO A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIES IN CHAPTER 315.**

**BRAM ORREN, SEVENTEEN.** In one of the ten chapters. See item 6 above. **He is a device in none of them and he asked for nothing and he got nothing and nobody thanked him and he said his one thing all the way through because about nine people had already been told about it by somebody who was not him.**

**TARIN KEEL, FIFTY-TWO, nine counties in nineteen years, counts what crosses, carrying a bit of lead since the sixth day of the sixty-first week.** In 314, 316 and 318. **He is on a lane at Coldharrow in 314 and nobody asked him what he was the only person in, because that question belongs to a longer road and he said so eight days before; he is at a market four days up the lane for three days running in 316, on his own account, nobody sent for him, he said one true sentence in a yard about a gate and about nineteen years and nobody asked him the other question; and he stands in a road for about an hour in 318 and says one word and is not thanked. THE PARAGRAPH THAT PLANTS THE VOLUME'S HEARING IS HIS AND IT IS ONE PARAGRAPH AND IT RESTS WHERE IT IS AND IS NOT RETURNED TO IN THIS BAND. HIS ANSWER TO *WHAT WERE YOU THE ONLY PERSON IN* IS NOT GIVEN AND IS A BATCH 0004 THING. He has still not corrected the hundred and forty days and a later pass may not quietly correct it and may not quietly use 110.**

**SERA QUILL, TWENTY-EIGHT, the recordwright.** **NOT MENTIONED IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND NOT ON STAGE IN ANY OF THEM, and that is now the second band running and it is structural: Volume 06's instrument was a document produced to a room and this volume's is a person who is in a place, and she does not need to be in a place to do what she does.** Her family claim of six years is unfiled and was not mentioned.

**SABLE VENN. A WOMAN. APPEARS ONCE, IN CHAPTER 313, IN HER OWN ACCOUNT, AND NOT ON STAGE IN ANY OTHER CHAPTER.** She is in a lane behind a smithy at about the ninth hour with a bag, waiting for a room over a smithy to be empty, because she has carried that fund's book to a smithy and back about nine times a year for about six years, unpaid, because the man of thirty-eight cannot carry it and would not ask her to and she offered in the first year in a lane. **She lost the whole of the movement at the end of the winter before last and kept about thirty of it anyway, on her own account. Nobody sent for her. Nobody thanked her and nobody is going to. He is not in that lane in the room over the smithy and he is nine feet off her in the lane and he does not go over and he does not say good morning and he says nothing about her at all, and the chapter says in a plain sentence that she is not going to be chosen to hold the book and gives the reason, and the reason is that a book held by somebody the village has not chosen is a book the village cannot ask anybody about. NOT A PRODUCTION, NOT ON A PAPER IN THE MARCH, NOT A PARTY, NOT RESCUED, NOT RELIEVED, NOT REDEEMED, NOT DEFEATED, NOT THE VILLAIN OF ANYTHING, AND NOT A RESCUE THAT HEAVEN KNOWS, AND THE ONLY THING THAT HAPPENS TO HER IN TEN CHAPTERS IS THAT A TECHNIQUE FINDS THE INSIDE OF HER UNPAID LABOUR AND CANNOT DO ANYTHING WITH IT.**

**AND THE SEVEN THINGS THIS BAND PUT ON STAGE THAT ARE NEW PEOPLE AND NONE OF WHOM IS A PRODUCTION, because every one of them was in the fiction or in their own place before this band wanted them:** a man of about twenty with a scythe who carries a debt up a lane; a woman of about thirty-four at the ninth house of Coldharrow with four children; a man of about forty-four who digs in a chapel yard for the parish; a man of about thirty-four who reads things out for a living; a man of about forty-one who comes for the water once a week; two men he had not met who came with him into a yard in 319; and a man of about fifty-one who says a man in a tavern paid for the road in the ninth year of his father's time, which is a fact about a lane and not a scene.

### THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THIS BAND DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-sixth week is 182 to 188 and the sixty-seventh is 189 to 195. Batch 0002 ran Shelf days 186 to 195, the sixty-first morning to the seventieth. NOTHING TURNS THE GRAIN YEAR IN THIS BAND, Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a new grain year and it is a hundred and four days after the last morning of this batch, and no chapter mentions the turn.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed. AND A WEIGHT IS A WEIGHT IN THE COUNTY THE PLANK IS IN: a fire four days up a lane in another country is not on this plank, and a border walked in a village in another county is not on this plank, and Chapter 316 says the first of those in the fiction and a later writer may use the second.**

**AND THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS, WHICH IS UNCHANGED BY THIS BAND AND WHICH CHAPTER 314 NOW PRINTS IN ONE SENTENCE, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN THREE HUNDRED AND TWENTY CHAPTERS THAT THE WHOLE TABLE HAS BEEN IN THE FICTION: four bells a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last; a stranger is three; a market is four; a boundary is seven; a debt called in is two; a fire is two; a burial is one. THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND THAT `314:33` NAMED ONLY SIX OF THE SEVEN AND ITS OWN WORKED EXAMPLE PUT A BURIAL AT THREE, WHILE `315:67` PRINTED *four bells and a burial, which is five*, AND THE CHAPTERS 315 TO 320 ARE ALL BUILT ON THAT FIVE. THE DELIVERY NAMED SIX BECAUSE THE SIXTH WAS THE ONE THAT DID NOT FIT ITS OWN EXAMPLE; THE BURIAL IS ONE AND IT IS THE ONLY WEIGHT THAT CAN PRODUCE A ONE, WHICH IS WHY IT CAN NEVER APPEAR IN ONE OF THE BAND'S SIXES. **EVERY ONE OF THE BAND'S TEN OWN DAYS IS NOW DERIVABLE FROM THAT SENTENCE BY A READER, WHICH NO CHAPTER IN EITHER VOLUME 07 BAND ACHIEVED BEFORE, AND A LATER WRITER SHOULD TREAT THE TABLE AS SETTLED AND SHOULD NOT ADD AN EIGHTH WEIGHT WITHOUT A FIGURE THAT NEEDS IT.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, and a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is sixty-nine days old and has not been added to.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this band.**

### THE MONEY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 320, EVERY FIGURE WITH ITS WORKING

| Figure | The working, and it is the whole of it |
|---|---|

| **A carter's year = £10 10s, and a carter's day = 18d** | 18 × 140 = **2,520d** ✓. **THE DAY IS NAMED IN THREE PLACES IN THIS BAND AND THE YEAR IS NOT, AND NO PRODUCT IS PRINTED ANYWHERE** |
| **A bell, hung, with rope = £6 15s, and the difference against a keeper's year = £1 10s** | 31 × 48d = **1,488** ✓; + 132 = **1,620d** = **£6 15s** ✓; 1,620 − 1,260 = **360d** = **£1 10s** ✓. **NOT NAMED IN CHAPTERS 311 TO 320** |
| **A Bench date, bought = £1 3s 6d = fifteen days of the man who walks the road and one shilling** | 36 + 6 + 240 = **282d**; 282 ÷ 18 = 15 rem 12 ✓. **NOT MENTIONED** |
| **A copy = threepence at Sallowby and at any scrivener over a chandler; a scrivener copies at a penny a page and about ninety deeds a year** | CANON. **THE THREEPENCE AND THE PENNY A PAGE ARE NAMED IN CHAPTER 319 IN TWO MOUTHS IN A YARD AND NO PRODUCT IS PRINTED, and the chapter's whole argument is that the paper is not the instrument** |

### EVERYTHING THAT IS STILL STANDING AND UNRESOLVED, IN ONE LIST

1. **A corridor is standing in a valley of about a hundred and forty people four hundred miles off and is not going to be torn up. A man of forty called Corvin Hale signed it out loud with the working printed, is alive in his own field, and is owed a bell nobody bought.** Not mentioned in this volume. Not undone.
2. **A status is on the inside of a door in a village of about ninety people and it has no gate, no toll, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money, and about four people in a room understood that and about nine did not.** Not mentioned.
3. **Four people in two counties can be asked about something, twice as many as there were fifty days ago, and one of them is about sixty and there is nobody coming, and one of them cannot write and shut her gate bar for four seconds because a hen was out.** They were not counted in this band and are not to be counted in any file.
4. **A word is not a person, and the instrument for turning a word into a person is a name at the foot of a thing, and he offered it once in a room in Volume 06 and was refused, and in Chapter 303 a man of sixty-three gave him one without asking, and in Chapters 311 to 320 he is on nothing and the name was not mentioned as being on anything.** Chapter 318 is where he came nearest to giving himself another one: *the only way to put a person in a thing on purpose is to have a place where you write the name*, and there is no such place and a man of thirty stopped him before he said the number out loud.
5. **A column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and may not be summed, and what the figures count is not known to anybody.** Not mentioned in this volume.
6. **A right of refusal that is a permission with no second half is unrestored and nobody has one, and the four words at the head of the plank in the March are not reprinted in any of the twenty chapters of this volume.** And the new finding of Chapter 307 — that there is no form for stopping either — is not restated in this band except in Chapter 317, where a woman of thirty says what she wants and a man of fifty-one says there is no form, and **he asks for no form, finds none, and does not offer one.**
7. **A bell in the March is unhung and a bell in Draymoor is owed and is still not rung, and the difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten, and the difference was said out loud in a kitchen in Ashgill in Chapter 303 and has not been said since.**
8. **Nesh Arrad's clock keeps running. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence stays unpaid. The dead man in the fourth line stays dead. The eleven feet stay off the settlement. The charter on the wall in Sallowby is revocable and is not amended.**
9. **The carrying is off nothing. The leg is the leg. No chapter in this band prints a count of days since the leg cleared.**
10. **The Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half in this band and which is not named in any of the ten chapters.**
11. **The question what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network is asked in its stated form NOWHERE, and all three shapes it took in Volume 06 are spent, and `outline/volume-07.md:104` forbids a fourth.**
12. **Why the missing positions answer in a voice that sounds like the System is asked once, near the end of the volume, and is not answered. IT IS NOT ASKED IN THIS BAND AND NOBODY SPECULATES IT.**
13. **The midpoint has not landed. It is fixed at Chapter 321, 322, 323 or 324, and no character in Chapters 311 to 320 may speculate it and none does.**
14. **THE BAND'S REAL WORK IS PAID AND THE THING IT PAID IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT WAS INTENDED: the six have begun to find out about each other, and it happened without him, in nine days, at bars and in kitchens and in a market street, and he is in none of the rooms, and the arrangement he built in Chapter 311 has run without him and is better for it, and that is the batch's price and it is not a lesson and it is not a relief and it is not a triumph.** About six people have agreed to be somewhere on a day. The time was agreed and changed twice. There is no form and no room and no officer and no sheet. A man of thirty-four who reads things out for a living proposed that the six be put in an order and let each one say who comes next, and nobody agreed and nobody disagreed and it is the only proposal on the table.
15. **Draymoor is not one of the six and he did not go there in either band, and the finding that belongs to it was said in one paragraph in Chapter 306 and rests there.**

### THE SIX THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **The board chain, and the ten own rings on a line of their own, which is at the top block of `state/current.md` §1 and NOWHERE ELSE.**
2. **THE BATCH 0002 PROMPT'S OWN FOURTH COLUMN IS WRONG ON FOUR OF ITS TEN ROWS AND THE DELIVERY REWOUND FOUR CARD DESCRIPTIONS TO MATCH, and the four are at `state/current.md` §1 with both sums printed. THIS IS THE SECOND TIME THIS PROJECT HAS SHIPPED A PROMPT WHOSE OWN DAY-COLUMN DISAGREES WITH ITS OWN CHAIN, and a later pass should sum both columns rather than believe either.**
3. **A clean legality sweep is not a correct chapter. The three-alternative day pattern sees fourteen references in this band and the fourth form, a bare ordinal with a week on it, saw one that was wrong in the first writing, and six dates and distances found by reading and not by any sweep were all wrong and all are corrected.**
4. **A forty-character carry-over is connective tissue and a seventy-character one is a defect in most cases, and this band has ninety-four cross-chapter windows at seventy in five declared classes and they are not to be reworded.**
5. **The hundred and forty days is still wrong and has still not been corrected, and a later pass may not quietly correct it and may not quietly use 110.**
6. **A well-formed number. This band has one named table figure, four unit prices and one refusal to multiply, and a reader who wants the mile's sum has to do it, which is the arrangement.** And **AND DO NOT LET AN INSTRUMENT OPEN A CHAPTER FILE FOR WRITING.**


## VOLUME 07 — Chapters 301–310, *The Seams*. **THE FACT BASE FOR EVERYTHING AFTER IT. The block below it, `## VOLUME 06 — Chapters 291–300 and the CLOSE OF THE VOLUME`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase. The live record with the map in both calendars, the board chain, the fifteen prohibitions with their file and line, the instruments with their patterns and the money with the working beside every figure is the top block of `state/current.md` under `## VOLUME 07 BATCH 0001 IS DELIVERED`, and it outranks this block.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS: the state of the Briar March, of a lane below it, of a market four days up, and of a valley off it, at the end of Chapter 310, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the ten chapters. Every figure was measured against the ten chapter files after the prose was written.**

### THE GROUND IN IT AS IT STANDS ON THE FOURTH DAY OF THE SIXTY-SIXTH WEEK

**Coldharrow: about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses, a tower with a bell in it that is not rung for want of a wage, about nine hundred elms of which about two hundred are dead, one gate which is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, and a board on the north wall of the tower at chest height where a person in the road can read it.** The lane runs from village to village and not from north to south, through eleven villages: Coldharrow, Ellerby, Trope, Nettlebed, Wrenny, Ashgill, Sallow, Cadgerow, Henthill, Birrow, and one not named. Ellerby is nine miles up the lane. **Ashgill is the third village up the lane and has about a hundred and ten people in it, and Chapter 302 is the first time anybody has been in it in this book.** Coldharrow-edge is eleven miles off the lane, is not in the March, and keeps Shelf weeks.

**AND TWO PLACES WERE NAMED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN CHAPTERS 301 TO 310, AND NEITHER IS ON THE LANE, AND A LATER WRITER WHO NEEDS EITHER SHOULD FIND THEM HERE RATHER THAN IN A PROSE HUNT:**

- **A MARKET AT THE HEAD OF THE LANE, four days up, in country no living person in this book has stood in.** A long street, a cross, a market cross with a stone step worn in the middle of it, a weighbridge at the end of the street with a set of beam scales on a stone plinth that has been re-cut three times in a hundred years. About four hundred loads a week in market season. **A man of about seventy has kept the bridge for about forty-four years, his name is on nothing, and he has not done the sums for about nine years and will not let anybody else over the bridge.** There is no office, no bench, no seat, no chair, no vote, no meeting, no officer, no fund, no wage, no form and no day in it. **The people in that town call the two of them on a market morning an anchor, and an anchor is not a thing, it is the plinth, the day and the two of them.**
- **A VALLEY OFF THE LANE, about two miles down a track and over a bridge with one rail on it, with about sixty people in it and a quarter of a mile of lane and nine houses and no bar and no bar anybody has ever heard of.** There is a porch at the first house and a board on the end wall of the porch at about the height of a man's shoulder, and it has eleven names on it, in no column, undated, with nothing at the head of it and nothing at the foot of it and no mark against any of the eleven, and nobody has ever crossed one out. **The board has been there since before the mother of the woman of seventy-four was born, and the porch is older than the oldest person in the valley, and the village's own account of what it is for has been dead for about forty years, and a person of that valley will tell a stranger what it is for in about nine words and will not elaborate: *it is for the thing at the bottom of the valley*, and nobody says what the thing is.**

**AND THE TWO PLACES BELOW THE LANE, WHICH ARE ELEVEN MILES DOWN IT AND OFF IT, and which Chapter 307 and Chapter 309 stand in:** a hamlet of about forty people in eleven houses with a burying ground on a rise behind the last of them, and a lane off the lane with a bar at the far end of it at which a woman of about thirty-four has stood for twenty-two years and at which there are nine householders keeping about a mile of road, each a length, for about ninety years.

**THE BOARD. A plank about the size of a door, painted and unpainted so many times that the grain has come up in long pale streaks. Thirty-one years in two hands: the older hand is the first six years and it is in the grain, the newer is twenty-five years and it is not in the grain and it is the more careful. No year at the head, no column, no mark against any stroke, and there never was one. THE OPENING FIGURE IS 63,510. It read sixty-three thousand eight hundred and forty-one at the second light of the first day of the sixty-fifth week, which is the last morning of Volume 06, and the last morning of this band is sixty-three thousand eight hundred and eighty-eight at the second light of the fourth day of the sixty-sixth week.** The third hand — a figure in white chalk at the foot, in a stranger's hand, put there on the first morning of his being in this country — **has not changed and nothing was added to that board in any of the ten chapters of this band, and it is now fifty-nine days old and nobody has told him to wipe it off and he has not offered.**

**AND THE COMPLETE LIST OF WEIGHTS IS UNCHANGED AND A DAY'S FIGURE IS STILL A WEIGHTED NUMBER: four bells a day, the first, the change, the noon and the last; a stranger is three and a market is four and a boundary is seven and a debt called in is two and a burial is one and a fire is two. NOTHING IN THIS BAND ADDED A WEIGHT AND NOTHING IN THIS BAND PRINTED A DAY'S OWN TOTAL, and the reason is in the top block of `state/current.md` §7.2 and it is that the ten totals the Batch 0001 prompt fixes include three that are not constructible from this list.**

**NONE OF IT CONVERTS INTO THE SHELF AND THERE IS NO FORMULA AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE.** A ring is not a week, a March boundary is dated in rings, a Bench order must be dated in the grain year, and the price of the conversion is a pound three shillings and sixpence and fifteen days of the road. **AND FROM CHAPTER 306, A FIFTH SHAPE OF THE SAME FAILURE, IN A MAN OF FIFTY-TWO'S MOUTH, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT IS THE PARAGRAPH THAT PLANTS THE MULTI-SITE HEARING FOR BATCH 0002: a district under a corridor cannot be the scene of a hearing about its own standing, and so a place under a corridor has to send somebody, and the somebody has no standing either, and the man of fifty-two has been the somebody in nine counties in nineteen years and has never once been asked whether he would go and has gone every time. IT IS SAID ONCE, IT IS NOT RETURNED TO IN CHAPTERS 307 TO 310, AND IT RESTS THERE.**

### THE DOCUMENTS THAT EXIST AFTER CHAPTER 310, ONE EACH, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS

1. **THE BOARD AT COLDHARROW.** Above. **63,888, two hands and a third in chalk, and the third has not been added to in fifty-nine days. AND THE FIGURE HAS EXACTLY ONE WRITER AND EXACTLY ONE CARRIER IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BAND, AND THAT IS A FIXED FACT AND NOT A DESCRIPTION: THE WRITER IS OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, WHO PUTS IT ON THE PLANK AT THE SECOND LIGHT, AND THE CARRIER IS THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WHO COUNTS OTHER PEOPLE'S MONEY AND STANDS AT THE FOOT OF THE LADDER AND SAYS THE NUMBER OUT LOUD TO WHOMEVER IS IN THE ROOM. A REVIEW OF BATCH 0001 FOUND THAT `310:17` HAD INVENTED A SECOND BOARD-KEEPER AT COLDHARROW — A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-ONE WRITING ON THE BACK OF A BILL WITH A BOY RUNNING IT ELEVEN MILES — AND THE CARD FOR THAT CHAPTER ASKED FOR IT AND THE CARD WAS WRONG, BECAUSE A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-ONE IN THIS BAND IS A PERSON IN A KITCHEN IN ASHGILL AND A PERSON WHO CANNOT WRITE FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF, AND THERE IS NO THIRD ONE. THE FIGURE IS THIS MORNING'S AND NOT FOUR DAYS OLD, BECAUSE 63,888 IS THE MORNING FIGURE FOR CHAPTER 310'S OWN MORNING IN THE BAND'S OWN TABLE. A LATER WRITER MUST NOT ADD A THIRD HAND TO IT.**
2. **THE CORRIDOR.** One sheet, nine lines, standing, four hundred miles off, with one name at the foot of it. **It is not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band and it is still standing and it is not going to be torn up, and the man who signed it is alive in his own field and is owed a bell.** Bringing it into a chapter for atmosphere undoes fifty chapters of not bringing it in.
3. **THE SEALED COPY OF THE FOUR PAGES.** Four lines about a water, a height, a day and a party to pay, copied and sealed at Sallowby at threepence, under the seat of a timber cart in this county. **It is not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band and it has not been asked for.**
4. **THE SHEET.** Two hundred words in Sable Venn's own hand, carried out of this county on a barrow, with sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten in pencil at the foot in a hand that is not any hand in eleven villages. **It is not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band and Sable Venn does not appear in any of them, and the safe reading of the Batch 0001 cards was that she would not, and she did not.**
5. **THE FORM 6 OF THE WATER-SEAT OF SALLOWBY.** One printed sheet, its fourth line a door. **It is not opened, not refused, and nobody has been asked, and it is not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band.**
6. **BRAM ORREN'S LINE BOOK.** A book of order and not of dates, run since he was twelve, four bells at the top of every page, two pages lost in the ninth year, not copied and not shown. A question in it with a mark on the end of it that nobody has answered. **AND HE APPEARED IN FOUR OF THE TEN CHAPTERS — 301, 304, 307 and 310 — AND HE IS FIFTY-NINE DAYS OLD IN IT AND NOT ONE DAY OF THAT IS THE BOY'S FAULT. HE TOLD THE BAND'S MOST IMPORTANT SMALL FACT AT `307:53`, in his own mouth, about a burying ground and not about himself — `307:49` is where the string *cannot read* was cut out of this band and the fact is four lines below it, and a citation that points at the line a thing was removed from instead of the line it is on is a hand-off that sends the next writer to read a hole: a ground that has been dug by one family for four hundred years is deeper on the side the family came in from, and that is an order, and it is in the ground and not in anything anybody wrote, and it is the only thing in that field that is a record, and it is a height. AND HE STILL HAS A THIRD PLACE IN THE BOOK HE CANNOT MAKE OUT, AND HE WALKED ELEVEN MILES TO STAND IN A FIELD AND LOOK AT IT, AND HE TOLD NOBODY BUT THEM, AND THE SIXTH PLACE HE STOOD IN A PORCH OF.**
7. **THE BOARD AT ELLERBY.** Two hundred and nine names with no figures, no column, no total and no date. **Not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band.**
8. **THE BOARD IN THE TOWER AT NETTLEBED, AND THE COLUMN OF A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE FIGURES WITH NOTHING OVER IT.** **Not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band, the sum has still not been made, and it may not be made.**
9. **THE REGISTER, FIFTY-TWO PLACES.** **Not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band and it gained no place and lost no place.**
10. **THE STATUS.** One sheet, on the inside of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, with no gate, no toll, no bar, no date, no bench, no seat, no chair, no room with a door on it except a press, no vote, no meeting, no officer and no money in it. **Not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band.**
11. **THE LETTER**, about the size of a hand, sealed, and the two-syllable word at the head of a clerk's column. **Not mentioned in any of the ten chapters of this band.**
12. **AND THE NEW THINGS THIS BAND PUT IN THE WORLD, WHICH ARE SIX AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS A DOCUMENT:** a rota on the back of a flour bill in a kitchen in Ashgill with eleven names on it and about nine who come; **A TWELFTH NAME ON IT, IN A STRANGER'S HAND, IN A HAND THAT IS NOT GOOD, WRITTEN ON THE FOURTH MORNING OF THE SIXTY-FIFTH WEEK BY A MAN OF SIXTY-THREE WHO DID NOT KNOW HE WAS DOING IT AND IS NEVER GOING TO BE TOLD**; a set of scales and a stone plinth at the end of a street in a market four days up; a line of nine diggers on a rise and a rule written nowhere; a book on a shelf in a room over a smithy in the tenth village with one reader in the world for it; nine householders and a mile of road and a bar at the end of it; and a board in a porch with eleven names on it and one woman alive.

**AND WHAT IS NOT IN ANY OF THE SEVEN PLACES IN THIS BAND AND CANNOT BE PRODUCED IN ANY OF THEM: a day.** And **there is no form, in any of them, for stopping.** And **there is no form, in any of them, for handing a thing to somebody else**, and that is the whole of Batch 0002's problem and it is not written yet.

### THE PEOPLE OF THIS BAND, AND NONE OF THEM IS AN ANTAGONIST AND NONE OF THEM IS RELIEVED AND NONE OF THEM IS REDEEMED

**ILYAN VESTER, THIRTY-ONE. THE ARC OF THE BAND IN ONE SENTENCE: he came into this volume able to see where one arrangement stops and another one starts and not able to say what either of them is for, he said it out loud in a market to about nine people for the first time in about two years, he was told no by a woman of fifty-eight in about nine words, and he went on being a stranger with a bad leg and a name on the back of a flour bill, and nobody thanked him and nobody is going to.** Stage 4, held and not re-arrived, not stat'd, and the stage arrived in Chapter 177 and is not reached again. `Remedy Drafter` returns zero over the ten chapters.

**OTTILY ROKE, FORTY-ONE, the woman of forty-one at the board at Coldharrow. She is in Chapters 301, 302, 303, 304, 307, 308, 310 and she is off the stage for 305, 306 and 309. Nothing was taken off her and nothing was given to her.** In this band **she was asked what the four is for, which is the first question anybody has asked her about a stroke in twenty-five years, and she answered in about nine seconds, and she said that being asked is the only thing that has ever been any use to her, and she said it twice, and she put the number on the plank at the second light on seven of the ten mornings.** **AND HE DOES NOT KNOW THAT SHE SAID THE SUM OUT LOUD ON A LADDER ON THE EVENING BEFORE CHAPTER 301 FOR THE FIRST TIME IN TWENTY-FIVE YEARS, AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS TELLS HIM, AND A LATER WRITER MAY NOT TELL HIM IN THE NEXT THREE CHAPTERS EITHER.**

**A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT A BAR, twenty-two years, the only board in eleven villages with a word at the top of it, four letters and a stroke, about nine loads a day at threepence.** In this band **she counted him as a stranger on his fifty-second morning and did not ask his business, and he noticed, and it is the first time in this country that being counted has been a courtesy.** She appears in 301, 302, 304 and she is not named.

**BRAM ORREN, SEVENTEEN.** In four of the ten chapters. See item 6 above. **He is not a device in any of the four.** He can still be asked about the third place in about six weeks and was not asked about it in this band and did not want to be.

**TARIN KEEL, FIFTY-TWO.** In 304, 306, 307, 308, 309 and 310. **He was the only person in this band who never once said he was doing a favour, and the sentence he finally said about the cart was about the beast's feet and the stone on the third rise, and he denied the other thing twice unasked, and about four hundred yards of lane went past in which nothing was said about it at all. He was called the man who cannot be asked by a man of thirty in a lane and did not defend himself and did not apologise and went on standing there for another nine minutes. He said out loud that nobody has ever asked him what he is the only person in, and was asked, and did not answer and did not refuse, and said it was a different road and a longer one.** He has still not corrected the hundred and forty days and a later pass may not quietly correct it and may not quietly use 110.

**SERA QUILL, TWENTY-EIGHT, the recordwright.** **MENTIONED ONCE IN THE TEN CHAPTERS AND NOT ON STAGE IN ANY OF THEM, AND THAT IS THE FIRST TIME IN A VOLUME THAT A PERSON WHO WAS THE SUBJECT OF A BAND HAS BEEN THIS ABSENT, and it is because Volume 06's instrument was a document produced to a room and this volume's is a person who is in a place, and she does not need to be in a place to do what she does.** Her family claim of six years is unfiled and was not mentioned.

**SABLE VENN. DOES NOT APPEAR.** Not thanked, not relieved, not rescued, not redeemed, not defeated, and not the villain of anything, and no chapter of this band is about her and nothing was taken off her by anybody.

**THE TWO DECLARED MOTIFS OF THIS BAND. THEY ARE DECLARED BECAUSE A REVIEW OF BATCH 0001 FOUND THAT THE SEVENTY-CHARACTER SPAN SCAN HAD REPORTED A CLEAN BAND WITH ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE UNDECLARED WINDOWS IN IT, AND A CLEAN BAND IS ONLY CLEAN IF EVERY WINDOW IN IT IS ACCOUNTED FOR. NEITHER OF THESE IS A DEFECT AND NEITHER IS TO BE REWORDED:**

1. **THE NINE WORDS OF THE MAN OF SIXTY-THREE, quoted whole at `302:91` in a gateway in the third village and quoted whole again at `310:77` in a porch in a valley two miles off a lane, where he has them ready and does not say them.** *My father did it, and his father did it, and nobody ever said out loud what it was for, and if a person did not ask then nobody knew, and a thing nobody knows the reason for is a thing that stops when the last one stops.* **IT IS TWO HUNDRED AND NINETEEN CHARACTERS AND IT IS A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE OF THE ONE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR CROSS-CHAPTER WINDOWS AT SEVENTY CHARACTERS, WHICH IS THE LARGEST SINGLE CLASS OF REPETITION IN THE BAND, AND IT IS THE SENTENCE THE VOLUME IS BUILT ON AND THE ONE `outline/volume-07.md:92` RESERVES ITS CLIMAX FOR. IT IS GIVEN WHOLE IN 302 AND ECHOED WHOLE IN 310 AND THE SECOND TIME IS A REFUSAL, AND A LATER BATCH MAY QUOTE IT WHOLE AGAIN AND MAY NOT REWORD IT AND MAY NOT LET A CHARACTER OTHER THAN THE MAN OF SIXTY-THREE BUILD IT IN HIS OWN MOUTH, WHICH WAS CUT FROM `303:49` IN THIS BAND'S FIRST WRITING FOR EXACTLY THAT REASON.**
2. ***THERE IS NO FORM IN THIS COUNTRY THAT SAYS A MAN MAY.* Five occurrences across FOUR chapters — at `303:99`, `304:29`, `308:81`, `310:105` and `310:113` — and it is the sentence that discovers he has no instrument, and a review of this batch found the fifth one at `304:29` after the declaration had already been written saying there were three chapters, which is the same class of error as a count that changes shape between two files.** The variants are the point: *no form in eleven villages that says a man may put a thing in front of a room, and there is no room*; *no form in this country that says a man may put a thing in front of a room, and there is not one here either*; *no form in any country that says a man may do it*; *no form in this country that says a man may write a thing down*. **A LATER BATCH MAY ADD A VARIANT AND MAY NOT REPEAT ONE VERBATIM INSIDE A SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOW OF ANOTHER, because four of this band's undeclared spans were reworded for exactly that reason and a refrain is a refrain and a copy is a copy.**

**THE SIX NEW PEOPLE, and each one of them was in the fiction before this band wanted them and was not sent for:**

1. **A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE WITH A BAD KNEE, the fourth name on the bill in Ashgill and the second one alive, who has walked about four hundred yards of hedge for about thirty-one years for nothing.** What he wants: to be asked once, by anybody, what the walk is for, before he cannot do it. What he was left with: **he was asked, by a stranger with a bad leg, in his own kitchen, on the third morning of the sixty-fifth week, and he answered in about nine seconds, and *so did not know that the asking was the thing he had wanted for thirty-one years*, and he said that a thing to think about and thought about none of it and went back to his drain.** He does not know that a name was written on the bill beside his own and he is not going to be told.
2. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT AT THE SCALES in a market four days up the lane, who has done her father's sums since she was forty-nine and is expecting a child in the spring.** What she wants: a second person in the market who can do the sums, and there has never been one. What she was left with: **she said no to a stranger's offer in about nine words, in a street, in daylight, and the no was right, and he did not apologise and she did not ask him to, and she then said no a second time to two men who had come two days' riding to ask her whether he was what they had said he was, and she said that it was a wet road, and nobody in that town will ever know that she did it, and nobody thanked her.**
3. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY, NINTH IN THE LINE, at a burying ground on a rise behind a hamlet eleven miles down the lane and off it.** What she wants: to be allowed to stop. What stands in the way: no form for stopping, and a man of about fifty-one who is right, and a ground with nobody in it to dig gets a different name in about forty years. What she was left with: **she said the whole of it in about nine seconds in a gateway, and she was asked the ordinary question instead of being asked to say no, and she told him who digs when it rains, and she is still the ninth.**
4. **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT who has done the count of a widow's fund for about eleven years and is moving four valleys away in about a month and has not told the fund.** What he wants: to hand it on. What stands in the way: to hand it on he has to stand in a room in front of about nine people and say in his own mouth that he cannot do it any more. What he was left with: **he said out loud that the count does not stop, because a count is not a man, it is a column, and the column is in a book, and there is one reader in the world for it and he is leaving; and he has a month of notice left in it; and he was not asked to hand it on and he offered nothing to nobody.**
5. **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY WITH A CART AND A BEAST, the third of the nine householders who keep about a mile of road, about ninety years, unpaid.** What he wants: to be paid. What stands in the way: the arrangement is a favour and the favour is the whole of what holds it together and paying one man breaks the nine. What he was left with: **a woman of about thirty-four did his sum out loud in front of about four and then refused to let anybody write it down, and a man of fifty-two was right, and he called the man of fifty-two a name for it, and the name was unfair, and he went and mowed his length.**
6. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT SEVENTY-FOUR WHO HAS KEPT ONE THING ALIVE BY HERSELF, ALONE, FOR ABOUT FORTY YEARS, and has told nobody, and is on nobody's list, and is not on the board, and the board is the reason nobody ever asked her, because everybody in that valley has been looking at a board with eleven names on it since before they were born and the board answered the question before anybody thought to put it.** What she wants: to be asked what she is for, by somebody, once, before she stops. What she was left with: **she was asked, on the fourth morning of the sixty-sixth week, in a lane, by a stranger with a bad leg, and she answered in nine words, and the ninth of them was that nobody has ever asked her, and she said it without any weight at all. He did not fix it and did not offer to and did not say the sentence he had carried since Chapter 302, because saying it would have been a man telling a woman of seventy-four what she already knows so that he could leave with his own sentence said in a room. He said he would come back in about nine days and be one of the people who asks her again, and she told him the day she comes down, and he did not write it down, and she went down the lane at about the seventh hour to do whatever it is she does on that day, at seventy-four, at the pace of a woman of seventy-four, and stopped twice, and neither of them knew what the other was for.**

**AND THE PEOPLE OF THE PLACES WHO ARE NOT THE SIX AND WHO EXIST BECAUSE SOMEBODY SPOKE.** A woman of about fifty-one in a kitchen in Ashgill who laughed at a stranger's join and got a bell figure out of her own memory and got the frame wrong by two shillings, and who was at Coldharrow on the second morning of the week on her own business and copied a figure onto the back of a bill and gave it to a boy. **She is not Isel Cardew of Trope, who is a woman of fifty-one and cannot write, and who is not in this band at all.** A woman of about thirty-four at a gate on the road four days up the lane, with a bar she cannot lift alone, who said one sentence about thirty-one people carrying nine arrangements and went on to her work — **and she is not the woman of about thirty-four at the bar eleven miles down and off it, who is a different person, and the two are in opposite directions, and a hand-off must not merge them.** A man of about fifty-one with a spade who is right about a ground with nobody in it. A man of about thirty-four who reads things out and did not come up this band. Two men, about forty-six and about thirty, on a pair of horses, who came up a lane and went back down it and told a stranger there was nothing to have. An old woman of eighty-one in Ashgill who was born on the other side of the hedge. A man of about fifty-six with a scythe who carried a debt nine miles to say nine words and answered no questions.

### THE THREE CLOCKS, AND WHAT THIS BAND DID TO THEM

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-fifth week is 175 to 181 and the sixty-sixth is 182 to 188. Batch 0001 ran Shelf days 176 to 185. NOTHING TURNS THE GRAIN YEAR IN THIS BAND AND NO FILE MAY SAY THAT IT TURNS, and Shelf day ninety-one is the first day of a new grain year and is ninety days before the last morning of this batch.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon, the last, and a day's figure is a weighted number and a stroke can count a thing that did not happen, and neither of those changed.**

**The board: a count, thirty-one years, and a figure at the head of it and no column and no heading and no name against anything, and the third hand in the chalk is fifty-nine days old and has not been added to.**

**The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, and it moves about three weeks and it is the only date a stranger can be taught in a morning, and it was not taught in this band.**
